# The RE2 oracle

RE2 has no reference source to build a tracer into, so `scripts/oracle-re2.py`
traces the original executable from outside and writes the same
`oracle-trace v1` file (`trace-v1.md`) as the RE1 reference.

It needs a retail install holding `bio2 1.10.exe`, Classic Rebirth's `ddraw.dll`
and `data/`, named by `--game` or `ARKLAY_RE2_ROOT`. On Linux it also needs
`wine`, `Xvfb` and, for screenshots, ImageMagick's `import`: the game runs on its
own virtual display, with a Wine prefix under `--work`. On Windows it runs the
game natively (see "On Windows" below). Either way nothing is written to the
install.

The virtual display is an `Xvfb` on the first number from `:100` with neither a
lock file (`/tmp/.X<n>-lock`) nor a bound socket (`/tmp/.X11-unix/X<n>`, by path
or abstract name), so a record never takes a desktop's `:0` or `:1`: an X server
unlinks its socket file when it exits, which would cut the desktop's new X
clients off. A number another server takes meanwhile is skipped (the second
server on a number fails to bind its abstract socket and exits without touching
the first one's socket file). The recorder's own servers, started with
`-displayfd`, write no lock file, so a lock is another X server's, and it blocks
its number even when the process it names is gone (it may be alive in another
PID namespace that shares `/tmp`). A socket file a killed server left behind
doesn't count, since the next server replaces it. However the record ends (done,
an error, Ctrl-C, SIGTERM or SIGHUP, or a start that fails before the game runs)
the server is terminated, not killed, so that it removes its socket, and it is
started with `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG`, so the kernel terminates it even when the
recorder itself is killed. A second Ctrl-C, SIGTERM or SIGHUP waits until the
clean-up is done. The tool reads `-displayfd` up to its newline before closing
the pipe (Xvfb writes the number and the newline separately and exits if either
write fails), then checks that the server is running and accepts a connection
before Wine starts. Xvfb's output goes to `xvfb.log` under `--work`, beside
Wine's `game.log`. A game that exits with code 13 before its first tick had no
Direct3D device, which under Wine means no display driver: the record stops with
"the game exited at start-up (code 13: …)", and the two logs say why.

```sh
# Record the original, run arklay on the same input, compare
scripts/oracle-re2.py scenario --input oracle/inputs-re2/street_walk.txt \
    --ticks 500 --out target/oracle-re2/street --pack re2.akpak

# Only record
scripts/oracle-re2.py record --ticks 500 --trace ref.trace

# Check that nothing but ticks decides what happens
scripts/oracle-re2.py determinism --ticks 1000 --out target/oracle-re2/det

# List the code Classic Rebirth changes
scripts/oracle-re2.py patches
```

`scenario` exits non-zero at any difference, as `oracle.py compare` does.

## How it works

The tool adopts the game's process and stops it with a breakpoint in `Pad_set`,
just after the call that reads the pad (`0x004D0F58`). Every tick passes there
once, and it's the only code the tool touches. At each stop it reads a line of
state from memory and puts the scripted pad value in `EAX`, so input goes through
the game's own mapping, edge detection and key repeat.

Ticks are counted, not timed. `determinism` records the same input twice, the
second time with a delay on every tick, and needs the two traces to be identical.

## On Windows

On Windows (`sys.platform == "win32"`) the same commands run the game natively, with no Wine, through the Win32 debug API (`scripts/oracle_re2_windows.py`, ctypes only, with Pillow for the pictures as on Linux). The game starts under `CreateProcessW` with `DEBUG_ONLY_THIS_PROCESS` from a tree of its own under `--work`: the executable and `ddraw.dll` copied, `data` a junction to the install's (a symbolic link needs a privilege on Windows), and `config.ini` with the same fixed settings as on Linux. The breakpoints (`BREAK_PAD` and `SsPlay`'s `BREAK_SOUND`) are `int3`s written with `WriteProcessMemory` and `FlushInstructionCache`, and are stepped over with the trap flag; `BREAK_PAD_BYTES` is checked as on Linux. The game is 32-bit and Python 64-bit, so the registers go through `Wow64GetThreadContext` and `Wow64SetThreadContext`, and the stops arrive as the WOW64 exception codes. As under ptrace, only the thread at `Pad_set` stops there: the tool takes its registers, suspends it and continues the debug event, so the game's other threads run while a stop's work is done. That matters with `--sound` (below). The game ends with the script, however it ends (`DebugSetProcessKillOnExit`), and the machine is kept from sleeping while it runs. The game's window opens on the desktop, without the focus.

Pictures come from Windows.Graphics.Capture, which hands over the window's own surface as DWM composes it: another window, or the system's (an update prompt, say), over the game doesn't change them, and the game's thread isn't asked for anything (`PrintWindow` waits for the window's thread, which is stopped at the breakpoint, and a copy of the screen shows whatever is on top). At a shot the tool waits for two compositions (`DwmFlush`) and takes the newest frame, cropped to the client area and placed at `FRAME_ORIGIN` on a 1024x768 image, so the files are the Linux path's. The game isn't DPI-aware, so it is started with the `HighDpiAware` compatibility layer, which keeps its client area at 640x480 on a scaled display, and the window's rounded corners (Windows 11) are turned off, since they would clip the picture's bottom corners. A minimised window has no picture, and DWM composes nothing while the display is off or the session is locked, so the display is kept on while pictures are taken, and a frame of play from a minimised window stops the record with a message, as do 300 pictures in a row with no new frame. Boot frames are skipped until the window is up.

`--sound` captures the game's own audio, and nothing else's, with process loopback (`VAD\Process_Loopback`, Windows 10 2004 and later), the counterpart of the PulseAudio sink: a 16-bit stereo 44.1 kHz `.wav`, each packet placed by its timestamp with silence in the gaps, so that the `.ticks` log's times line up with it as they do on Linux. The sound also plays on the speakers. The `.music` log and the `snd`, `bgm` and `xavol` fields are the same as on Linux.

With `DirectSoundEnable` on, a voice line ends by DirectSound's clock, not by ticks: `fg_status`'s `0x20` (and `0x20` in `0x130`) clears when it has played, so how many ticks a line holds depends on how much wall-clock time a tick takes, and Kendo's scene waits for its lines. On Linux and Windows alike, a `--sound` recording with pictures runs far slower than the game's 30 ticks a second (0.28 seconds a tick in the stored route, about 0.2 natively). A record that froze the whole game for a picture would hold the lines for more ticks still, which is why only the thread at `Pad_set` stops. So a `--sound` recording with a voice line in it is only reproduced on the ticks of another at that other's pace: `--pace TICKS_LOG` (on both systems) holds the thread at `Pad_set`, with the others running, until each traced tick is as far from tick 0 as it was in the recording whose `.ticks` log it is. Even then the bit can clear a tick or so apart, and two recordings at the same pace can differ there too: `determinism` with `--sound` over 1500 ticks of `door_101_102.txt` has `fgstat` differ on two ticks (1326 and 1449) and nothing else. Without `--sound` it is identical. Only runs that name `fgstat` compare it, and those with a voice line in their window only with `voice_from_ref`. arklay's `--trace --sound` takes `--voice-ticks FILE`, one tick count a line in the order the lines play, and holds each line's bit for that many ticks from its `xa_on` in place of its own length, so a run can follow a recording's pace without replaying it.

The game's fades are drawn off its own thread, on a clock (Classic Rebirth's, it seems): with the game stopped at `Pad_set`, the picture of a fade goes on changing for some 300 ms. So the frame of a tick in a fade depends on when it is taken, here two compositions after the stop and on Linux whenever `import` gets to it, and can be a step apart.

Recorded natively, the traces of the regression runner's references are byte for byte the Wine recordings of `item_take` (with `--sound` and every frame), `door_101_102` and `stairs_118_a`. `route_100_200` (with `--sound` and every frame, and `--pace` from its own `.ticks`) matches the Wine recording on every field of all 7400 ticks but `fgstat` on 14, at the ends of Kendo's lines (between 1310 and 2119) and one at 3457 to 3460, each a tick or three apart; without `--pace` its lines end later and the scene, and so the player, part from the Wine recording at tick 1331. `determinism` over 1500 ticks of `door_101_102.txt` without `--sound` is identical, and also identical to the first 1500 ticks of the Wine recording. The frames differ from Wine's by a mean of 0.012 to 0.016 per channel (out of 255, at 640x480) over `item_take`'s 1000 ticks of play and 0.031 over the route's 7400. The 2D frames of the status screen don't differ at all: what differs is the 3D, where native Direct3D and Wine's rasterise and filter edges and textures a little differently (a door's model during a load is the most, up to about 1.2, and the hall of room 200 up to 4.4), with the same poses in the same places, and the fades. Two frames of the route differ by more: tick 2, the original's first frame of the fade-in, shows natively, as in arklay, where Wine's is still black (29.7), and tick 3053, the first black one of room 102's door, is still the room natively (38.4); the route's other seven doors go black on the same tick on both.

## Ticks and input

Tick 0 is the first tick after the room's load has settled: a few ticks after
the player comes alive (`pl.life != 0`), which is when `Pl_init` has run and the
start position's collision push has landed. `--start leon` or `--start claire`
takes a fixed route there through the title screen: Original Mode, the
character, Normal, then confirm every 60 ticks until play begins. That takes 907
ticks for both, and they aren't traced. A line holds the state at the end of its
tick, and a pad value given for tick `t` first shows in line `t + 1`.

`--no-enemies` clears bit 0 of every enemy's `be_flg` at each stop, so nothing
runs them or collides with them. That leaves the player alone to compare with
arklay, which is how the routes are checked; the zombie recordings run with them
left on. `oracle/inputs-re2/` holds the scripts in use, and `street_wander.txt`
is a random walk through room 100.

`--keep-enemy SLOT` leaves that slot running, for a character arklay has. Room 101's scene wants `--no-enemies --keep-enemy 0`: the input `first_door.txt` over 4500 ticks, compared with `--fields room,cam,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,mot,em`, matches from the room's first line. On arklay's side, start as `door_101_102.txt` does below (`--room 100` with `door_101_102.lua`) with the input 780 ticks earlier, over 3720 ticks, and compare at `--offset 780`: every tick matches but `mot` until room 101's load at 825 and, after it, the standing clip's speed x until 1300, as there. A zombie left running would attack the player on the route, so this doesn't show the scene with them.

`kendo_attacked.txt` runs from room 100 through the door into room 101 and through the scene where the zombies break the window and kill Kendo, with the player left to die afterwards. `--no-enemies --no-enemies-room 0` clears the enemies in room 100 only (the number is `ROOM`'s, stage and room as `game.u16(ROOM)` reads them), and arklay starts at the door with a `--setup-script` and the input moved 780 ticks earlier (`--offset -780`, `--room 100 --player 0`). `rseed=` is `Sce_rnd_set`'s state. A door keeps it, so to compare this route tick by tick, force its value and `Rnd`'s in arklay's start with a setup script's `api:set_re2_sce_rnd` and `api:set_re2_rnd`, so that arklay's tick 0 has what the original has at that point. `rseed=` only shows the low 16 bits of the state and `set_re2_sce_rnd` takes all 32, so read them from the original's `RANDOM_BASE`. The state steps twice between the setup script and tick 0, and room 100's load draws `Rnd` 77 times, so the values to force come from before that point. Check the first ticks' `rng=` and `rseed=` against the original before trusting a run, and compare `em` and `zm` too: Kendo chooses where to turn from `Sce_rnd`. A setup script that does this places the player at `-20524, 0, -18869`, angle 2064, with health 200, `api:set_re2_rnd(39135)`, `api:set_re2_sce_rnd(2060504824)` and `api:set_room_camera(8)`, and clears room 100's zombies as the original's `--no-enemies-room 0` does with `api:set_re2_flag(6, N)` for their dead flags 35 to 41 (with them gone, the room's load draws no `Rnd`). Tick 10, where the door starts, prints a different `rng` and `rseed` for that tick only. `em`'s and `mot`'s last three fields are `Spd` and aren't compared. The player's neck turn (`neck=`'s fifth field) can differ by one for a tick, where the original's settles a tick later. With that, `rng`, `rseed`, the player, Kendo and all the zombies match over 3820 ticks, and `pixels` has a mean error of 1.21; the worst frames are the frames of two cuts. With `--every 1`, the frames through the door match too: the original shows black from the tick after the door fires, because `Psx_main` presents nothing of its hold, and after the door it skips the room's first four frames (`Swap_Cbuff`'s `Movie_set(4)`), so the fade-in shows from its fifth tick.

`door_101_102.txt` takes the same start through room 100's door, waits out Kendo's scene in room 101 (with room 101's zombies left on, since `--no-enemies-room 0` clears room 100's only) and walks to room 101's door to room 102 and through it. Record it with `record --no-enemies --no-enemies-room 0 --input oracle/inputs-re2/door_101_102.txt --ticks 3500`. On arklay's side, run `--room 100 --player 0 --character 0 --ticks 2720` with `--setup-script oracle/inputs-re2/door_101_102.lua` (the setup above) and `--input oracle/inputs-re2/door_101_102_arklay.txt` (the same input 780 ticks earlier), and compare at `--offset 780` with `--fields room,cam,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,mot,rng,rseed,em,zm,pneck`. Both doors switch on the original's tick, room 101 at 825 and room 102 at 3087, and everything matches to tick 3499 but for the two doors' own ticks (790 and 3052, `rng` and `rseed`) and the start: `mot` until room 101's load at 825, because `--room` starts room 100's clip on its first frame, and after it until the first step at 1300 only in the standing clip's speed x, 61 in the original, left by room 100's walk, and 0 in arklay, and `pneck` until 1129, because the head keeps where room 100 placed it until room 101 places it on tick 1130. With `--sound` on both sides the same holds over `--ticks 3700` and 2920, apart from `mot` until 915, room 101's load with the sound's hold, and `--fields room,bgm,xavol,snd` matches on every tick.

The other doors on the route are traced from a warp in front of each with the input `door_11b.txt`: `--warp 102:-25067:0:15229:2048`, `118:-24617:0:-24996:1024`, `119:13032:0:-19315:0`, `11A:9150:0:-2600:3072` with `--flag 6:156 --flag 6:157`, `11B:11393:0:-15351:3072` and `103:10303:-5400:-7823:0`, with `--life 200` over 900 ticks (1200 for 11A), and 100 more with `--sound`. arklay runs `--room` of the same room with a setup script that places the player where the original's tick 344 has them, with its angle, the camera cut the original has before play begins (`api:set_room_camera`, from its tick 343; see the stairs below), health 200, the flags and `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, and the input 344 ticks earlier; compare at `--offset 344`. Every one switches rooms on tick 399, and every field matches on every tick but `rng` on the door's tick, 364. The head (`pneck`) matches on tick 344 and differs on the six ticks after it, by up to 43 units in `z`.

`street_shoot.txt` aims the handgun in room 100 and fires until it's empty, with the zombies running. Over 2000 ticks, with the input long since ended, the zombies come for the player and kill them: damage, death and corpses, six grabs with bites and shove-offs, the player dying in a zombie's hands, the others eating the body and `Die`'s stops. Everything matches through the end of the game over at tick 1997 (`--fields rng,p.x,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,zm0,zm1,zm2,zm3,zm4,zm5`). The original then returns to the title. With `--sound`, `snd=` lists each tick's `SsPlay`s as `type:index:volume:pan`; without it the game has no audio device and plays none. arklay's trace has the same field, so sound is compared like the rest. `neck=` starts with the same flags, joint, whether it has a target and turn on both sides. `pixels` compares the game over's frames too: `YOU DIED` within a few levels of the original, and `die=` in the trace holds `Die`'s phase and the text's state.

`street_gun_low.txt`, `street_gun_level.txt` and `street_gun_up.txt` fire in room 100 at each aim. Run over 1500 ticks with weapon 5, 6, 7 or 8 equipped in both games and its ammunition given (`--equip WEAPON --give 0:WEAPON:MAGAZINE --give 1:AMMO:60`), all twelve fights match exactly on `rng`, the player and every zombie, including `Em10_Die`'s blown-off, torn and thrown deaths and the arm pulled off in `street_gun_up.txt` with weapon 5. `--equip` holds a weapon from the moment play is settled, and `--life` sets the player's life when play begins. The licker's id `0x22` is in `zm` too. Name the zombies `zm0` to `zm5` in `oracle.py compare --fields`: it silently ignores a bare `zm`. Frames from `pixels` differ by a mean of about 3.5, from the fires' animation phase and the pieces a blast throws (`Parts_bomb`).

The licker's behaviour moved from Rust to `enemy/22/enemy.lua` with byte-identical traces over 36 fights in room 211, and the zombie's to `lib/zombie.lua` the same way, over the street inputs here, room 101's attack, the feeding rooms and generated fights across the weapons and low life. When moving another enemy, compare traces of the old and new builds the same way: it needs no Wine and shows the first tick that differs. Run these on a variety of weapons and player life, and cover the damage reactions, since a short fight may not reach them.

The climbing licker was checked with `record` traces under weapon 2 and 200 life (`--equip 2 --life 200 --give 0:2:200 --give 1:0x14:60`), compared at `--offset 344` against arklay runs with the same input. These matched the original tick for tick, ignoring the speed fields:

- Room 204's ceiling licker, with `--warp 204:-14992:0:-12079:0 --flag 1:6` over 1300 ticks, fired at from tick 150.
- Room 10A's wall licker, with `--warp 10A:-19000:0:-6000:1024 --flag 1:1` over 2400 ticks, with Leon standing still. Six other 10A fights (aiming, shooting and walking past) also matched.
- Room 20E's wall licker, with `--warp 20E:-24200:0:-19000:0 --flag 1:1`, standing still. It matches for 2400 ticks; a walking run strays after about 600 ticks at `kara_rootck2` (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`).

`pixels` with `--warp 10A:-25000:0:-12000:1024:8` (camera 8) shows the wall licker and its shadow; the mean error over 400 ticks of aiming and shooting is 1.3. Room 204 at camera 5 shows the ceiling licker. The pose matches there, but the shading differs. Its arm's near-plane clip used to differ too; models are now clipped at the original's plane (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "Models at the near plane"). `leon_a_200_203` has frames over room 204's licker fight (its window 1291 to 1839), but no run has the licker near the camera at cut 5.

Room 200's entry scene, where the camera pans up the hall, plays from `--warp 200:-21700:0:-3700:1024 --flag 1:1 --life 200` with no input: the scene starts from cut 0. On arklay's side, `--room 200` with a setup script that places the player there, sets flag 1:1 and calls `api:set_room_camera(0)`. Compare at `--offset 344`. The player, the cuts, `Rnd`, `rseed`, `mot` and the inventory match the original for the scene's 356 ticks, and `pixels` had a mean error of 0.28. `Hokan_flg` stays 0 from tick 344 to 508: that is `Pl_init`'s, which sets `Move_no` 2 and clears `Move_cnt`, `Hokan_flg` and `Mplay` (0x4D998B), and the scene's `plc_stop` takes the player before `pl_mv_00` has run a tick, so nothing writes the flag until the scene hands him back on tick 509. arklay's `--room` start holds the stand's first tick already (its `Hokan_flg` is 6), so a `plc_stop` on the player before any tick has run the routines puts `Pl_init`'s 0 back. The regression runner's `scene_200` is this run, with `scene` too (1 until the scene hands the player back on tick 509), and without frames, which its reference doesn't have. The scene's `pos_set` puts Leon inside the entrance landing's box. The original's `Sca_ck_em` still checks the floor he came in on (`Floor`), not his height, so it pushes him out against his last position (`Old_x` and `Old_z`).

Room 200's computer (game mode 7, `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, the `sce_at` kind 14 entry) is checked from a warp into the counter computer's area, event 6's area 7: `--no-enemies --warp 200:-16200:-1800:2100:2048 --flag 4:6 --flag 4:7 --flag 4:102 --life 200`, where 4:6 skips the hall's entry scene and 4:102 keeps its event 5 from starting. The warp pushes Leon to `-14935, -1800, 2099`, which `computer_200.lua` (and its `_nocard` and `_released` variants) give arklay's `--room 200` start, with the inventory's every slot. The inputs press action at 364 (message 5, "Will you use the computer?"), answer yes at 400, which plays the decide cue (4:6), and then hold action for 4 ticks every 30, which confirms each of the console's lines once it has typed; the last press comes before the computer exits, since one after it would check the computer again. With the Blue Card Key (`--give 2:0x35:1 --give 3:0x26:1`, `computer_200_card`), game mode 7 starts on 401 and the computer on 402 (cut 15), the console types from 416, the card's question opens on 581 and its yes on 640 takes the card, moves the herb up and raises 4:70 on 641; cut 9 shows on 766, cut 2 on 886, cut 15 again on 1006, the close starts on 1061, and play comes back on 1075. `computer_200_nocard`, `computer_200_released` (`--flag 4:70`) and `computer_200_refuse` (a `right` before the press that answers) take the other branches. All four match the original on `room, cam, rseed, rng`, the player, `mot`, `inv`, `snd`, `fgcom` and `pc`, the computer's state, on every tick. Their frames match too: arklay draws the cut's background alone while the computer runs, and the console, its frame and its cursor over it. In `computer_200_card` the console's ticks (416 to 580, 641 to 765, 1008 to 1060), the door shots (768 to 1007) and the room after the computer (from 1076) are within 0.04 of the original's frames, the frame's steps are exact apart from 409 and 1070, where the original's frame repeats the step before (0.9 and 0.6), and the cut changes hold their two frames as `Swap_Cbuff`'s other loads do. The rest is the message windows: "Will you use the computer?" before game mode 7 and the card's question on 581 to 640 (mean 2.4). The four runs' frame means are 0.27, 0.18, 0.17 and 0.39 (card, no card, released, refused).

`licker_close.txt` and `licker_long.txt` fight the licker of room 211 with weapon 2 (`--warp 211:4000:0:-14400:2048 --flag 8:119 --give 0:2:90 --give 1:0x14:60`, 1844 and 3744 ticks). Compare at `--offset 344`, against an arklay run of `--room 211` with the input moved 344 ticks earlier and a `--setup-script` that places the player, calls `api:set_re2_flag(8, 119)` and `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`: a door keeps the stream, so the script sets the value it has when the room's play begins (`Rnd` is only seeded at a new game or at a door that changes the player's model). These runs equip the weapon and its 90 rounds with `--equip` and `api:set_equipped_weapon`: the large magazine avoids a reload. Fights with weapons 5 to 8 and with a low starting life match there too. With the licker in view, use `--warp 211:-500:0:-14400:2048:5` and `api:set_room_camera(5)` for `pixels`: the cut doesn't follow a warp, and frames there differ by a mean of about 0.5, with the tongue in view too. A shot at the licker's tick 120 of that run (`50..700 aim`, `120..125 aim fire`) draws the tongue at tick 169; the same with no shot never does, and a run that lets it kill the player (`50..900 aim`, `70..75` and `200..205 aim fire`) matches through the bite and the white-out for 700 ticks. A pack is loaded whole into memory, so run these one at a time. A reload's dropped magazine (`Mag_set`, `Mag_down`) matches `Rnd`, position and rotation at every tick with weapon 5 in that room and magazine size 8.

`licker24_fight.txt` and `licker24_close.txt` fight the two `0x24` lickers of room 605 with weapon 2 (`--warp 605:-24500:0:-24000:0 --give 0:2:200 --give 1:0x14:60 --equip 2 --life 200`, 1800 and 1900 ticks). Leon drops in from the duct. In the first he holds his ground; in the second he runs east to the wall and fires as they come. Compare at `--offset 344` against an arklay run of `--room 605` with the input moved 344 ticks earlier and a setup script that places the player at `-24500, 0, -24000`, angle 0, gives the same life and items, equips the weapon and calls `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`. The room's resting sparks (`Esp_tbl`'s 0x41) draw `Rnd` from the first tick, so they have to match too. `Rnd`, the player and both lickers match to the end of both runs: in `licker24_close.txt` one licker takes a shot and bites Leon five times. `pixels` over the same run has a mean error of 0.23. `pixels` runs arklay with `--no-mods`, so give it a pack converted with the scripts being tested, not a mod.

Room 201's licker scene in scenario A is `licker_201.txt` (`--warp 201:-25500:0:-21500:1024:2 --life 200`, 1000 ticks, and the same with `--start claire`): the player walks north from cut 2 and reaches cut 4 on tick 387, which starts `main_04`. The licker (`Type` 0x4402, so `Routine_1` 0x12) walks along the wall behind the window while the script shows its legs (parts 14 to 21), fades parts 0 to 21 from `0x707070` to black over 28 ticks with `parts_set` cases 0 and 1, and hides every part but 0 on its pass 28. It holds its pose from tick 433. Compare at `--offset 344` against `--room 201` with a setup that places the player at the warp, angle 1024, camera 2 and `Rnd` 6114; the regression runner's `licker_201` and `licker_201_claire` are these runs, with `zp` in their fields. Both match on every field for all 656 ticks, with a frame mean of 0.29 (Leon) and 0.25 (Claire) from the cut on, and the licker's pixels through the window darken as the original's do.

Room 204's licker scene (which Leon A and Claire A cross after the Blue Card) is `licker_204_scene.txt` (`--warp 204:-15400:0:-8000:3072 --flag 4:85 --life 200`, 1800 ticks, and the same with `--start claire`). A first entry with flag 4:85 clear runs `main_06`, which places the player at `4181, 0, -17026` with angle 2128 wherever the warp put them, so the run sets 4:85; flags 1:1, 1:6 and 4:86 must be clear. `main_02` puts the licker in slot 1 off the stage (`32000, -5700, 32000`, `Type` 0x4510, `Routine_1` 0x10). The player walks north from 360 into area 7, which starts `main_09` on tick 395 (cut 7). The scene's `movie_on 10` comes on tick 424 (Leon) or 425 (Claire); the next tick's `pos_set` and `member_set(23, 1)` move the licker over the window and let it go. It falls on clip 0x12 to -3108 (434), turns over and lands in one tick (240, then 0 on clip 0x13), stands on clip 3 (state 5) from 458, and the second `member_set(23, 1)` sends it to 0x0E on 459 and on into its fight. With no input after the walk, it kills the player by tick 1481 (Claire 1583). Compare at `--offset 344` against `--room 204` with `licker_204_scene.lua` (the warp's place, health 200, `Rnd` 6114, camera 0, flag 4:85). Both runs match on every field, the body in slot 0 (`zm0`) included, except the dead player's `pneck` on two ticks of the fall (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "What a climbing licker doesn't do"). `licker_204_fight.txt` is the same walk, then aim, fire and let go every 20 ticks from 470, with 2000 life and the handgun (`--equip 2 --give 0:2:200`, or 3 for Claire; 2400 ticks). Leon's run matches on every tick: on 918 a fire pressed as the aim starts is also the action key, so it checks area 6 (the body) and opens message 2, which stops the room for 39 ticks until the fire press on 958 answers it; on 1058 a second check runs event 8, whose `aot_on(4)` crouches him to the item under the body, and the fire press on 1178 answers the get-item window. Claire's run matches on every tick too, including tick 1962, where the licker drops from the ceiling beside her (`Routine_1` 0x0B): arklay used to push the two apart there, with Leon's box height for Claire (`Pl_init`'s 0x546 for her, "Claire A to the Diamond Key"). The licker isn't killed in either fight.

Room 207's zombies, which Leon A and Claire A both meet, are the first whose `Type` has neither 0x1000 nor 0x40: `init_00` places a 0x13 (slot 0, `Type` 6) and three 0x1F (`Model_type` 1; slot 1 `Type` 0, slots 2 and 3 `Type` 6) in scenario A, with sound types 44 and 42, a swapped pair (bank 0x38, so slot 0 plays the second half). Without 0x1000, `Root_ck` routes them by the room's two guide areas: area 0 (x -26700 to -22900, z -26600 to -6500), where the player arrives with slots 0 and 3, and area 1 (x -22900 to -13800, z -14000 to -9500), where slots 1 and 2 start and get the area's link point from their first `kara_rootck2`. The opt-in `zd` field traces each zombie's `Dest` (`+0x1c4`, `+0x1c6`): `zd=slot:x,z;...` or `zd=-`. `Em10_Init` runs in the room load and draws 29 `Rnd` (five for every id and three for each 0x1F's skin roll, which `Model_type & 0xC0` leaves on though `& 0x3F` picks skin 1), so the reference's `rng` is 55383 at 343 and 30861 at 344. The runs in `oracle/re2-regress.d/zombies_207.toml` warp to room 205's door 2 arrival (`--warp 207:-25640:0:-16590:0:1 --life 200`, and the same with `--start claire`) and compare at `--offset 344` against `--room 207` with `zombies_207.lua` (the warp's place, angle 0, health 200, `Rnd` 6114, camera 1). The warp leaves slot 2 with `Dest` -5586, 128 (Leon and Claire alike), which it walks toward on 344 before its first `kara_rootck2`: slot 1's model, loaded in the same room's load, ends under slot 2's work, and arklay's memory of the load has it too (docs/internals/deviations/re2.md, "What a zombie's slot held before"). `zombies_207_stand` and `zombies_207_stand_claire` stand still for 1200 ticks: slot 3 grabs on 487, and the grabs, bites and shoves take the player to 50 life. Both match on every field, `zd` included, for all 856 ticks. `zombies_207_fight` fights them from the same warp with the handgun (`--give 0:2:13 --give 2:0x14:39`, `zombies_207_fight.lua`): slot 3 is shot from the north; slot 0 takes a hit, falls toward Leon, takes hold of his legs as a crawler and dies at the hold's end; slots 2 and 1 each take hold of him with no press; slot 2's second hit, at 86 life, takes `At_sce_no` below 1, so `Em10Dm00` draws it again (a `Type` without 0x40, above 0x50 life); the empty gun reloads on a fire press; and Leon runs east to door 2 and room 208's load. It matches on every field, `inv` and `zd` included, for all 1716 ticks but the door's (1977's `rng`), and its frames by a mean of 0.84. It plays with sound, as it was recorded: `Snd_bgm_ck` holds the door's clear of the room's enemies and the load 90 ticks, so without sound arklay's room 208 has no zombie works on the ticks the original's still holds room 207's. `zombies_207_fight_snd` compares its sound, slot 0's from bank 0x38's second half, on every tick. No zombie reaches the herb pots, which hold the enemies out of their boxes as they do the player ("Script objects" in `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`). `zombies_207_grabs` (`oracle/re2-regress.d/zombie_grab_207.toml`, `zombies_207_grabs.txt`, the stand's setup) lets them take hold of a standing Leon and works the struggle (`Gacha_ch`, `Key_trg & 0x34F`: `Timer0` starts at 0x94 and loses 2 a tick, 7 on a fresh press; the zombie lets go when it goes below 0): left on every other tick from two ticks after grab 1 (487) frees him on 551, a 64-tick hold; left held through grab 2 counts once, so it lasts the full 105; left and right in turn on every tick of grab 3 (868) free him on 920, 52 ticks. START ten ticks into grab 4 and on the tick after its free is refused (`fg_status`'s 0x40, which `Pl_Em_damage` raises on every held tick), and START two ticks after grab 5's free opens the status screen. Grab 6's bite takes the last 10 life, so the zombie lets go at once, and grab 7's kills: the others come to eat, and the game over returns to the title on 2124. It matches on every field for all 1780 ticks, and `zombies_207_grabs_snd` on the sound.

The effect pool (`esp`, see "The trace") is checked on four of these recordings, made again with the field: `street_shoot.txt` from the new game, `water_walk.txt` and `spider_shots.txt` with weapon 2 in room 302, and `licker24_fight.txt`, each as its recipe above has it (`oracle/re2-regress.d/esp.toml`; room 302's spiders and room 605's lickers are `zm0` and `zm1`, and the tracer's `zm` lists id `0x24` too). On every one the recipe's own fields still match. The kinds and rows that are out and how many are live (`--fields espn,espk`) match on every tick of the first three, through room 100's fire, the shots' blood and flashes, the bodies, the eating at the game over (`FUN_00454670`'s blood on part 8, which arklay used to leave out), the footfalls' ripples, the spent cases' splashes and room 605's sparks; the slots themselves (`--fields esp`) part where an effect sits on a part's matrix (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "Attached effects").

`crow_shots.txt` fights the eight perched crows of room 109 in scenario B (`--warp 109:-14000:0:-14800:1024 --flag 1:1 --equip 7 --give 0:7:200 --give 1:0x14:60`, 2400 ticks). Compare at `--offset 344` against an arklay run of `--room 109` with the input moved 344 ticks earlier and a setup script with the same place, life, items, `api:set_re2_flag(1, 1)` and `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`. `Rnd`, the player and all the crows match to the end, and also with weapon 5 and with the wire crows and weapon 7 without the flag. With `:9` on the warp and `api:set_room_camera(9)` the same fight has them in view for `pixels`: the bodies and their poses match, with differences only along the edges of their wings.

`crow_held.txt` runs into the first crow with the gun raised and a turn held, so the peck lets go of the player straight into aiming and turning (same flags, items and setup script, 1500 ticks). A hold's last tick plays the crow's clip and then changes the player's bank (`FUN_00463210`'s release), and `Foot_set_pl`'s first turning tick after the blend reads that pose, so this shows whether the pose of a hold's end is right. `Rnd`, the player and all the crows match through tick 786. `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md` has what follows.

`dog_shots.txt` fights the two dogs of room 212 (`--warp 212:-11250:0:-25692:3024 --flag 4:83 --equip 2 --give 0:2:200 --give 1:0x14:60`, 2400 ticks). Compare at `--offset 344` against an arklay run of `--room 212` with the input moved 344 ticks earlier and a setup script with the same place, life, flag, items and `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`. `Rnd`, the player and both dogs match to the end. So do fights with weapons 3 and 5 to 8, standing still until killed, running and shooting, and rooms 21A (sleeping and eating, kinds 7 and 8), 20F (kind 4) and 216 (kind 2, flag 1:6). The exceptions are in `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`. The first trace row of a dog in 21A differs in its speed, which the original hasn't set yet. Weapons 4, 10, 11 and 14 can't be tested this way: arklay doesn't aim weapon 4 yet, and with the others the reference run ends early. `pixels --arklay-input` gives arklay its own moved input, and these frames differ by a mean of about 0.8, mostly in the player's shot and a blast's parts.

Mr. X (`Em_2A`, `enemy/2a/enemy.lua`) is in `zm` too. In scenario B, room 20D has him walk at the player from the far end: `--warp 20D:-26510:0:-24891:3752 --flag 1:1 --flag 4:78 --life 200` with no input, against an arklay run of `--room 20D --character 2` with a setup script that places the player, sets the life and both flags and calls `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`. Compare at `--offset 344`. `Rnd`, the player and Mr. X match from his first step to the game over. With `mrx_shots.txt` (a shot every 30 ticks) or `mrx_shots_slow.txt` (every 60), `--equip 7 --give 0:7:200 --give 1:0x14:60`, and the same items, `api:set_equipped_weapon` and the moved input on arklay's side, the fights match to the end with weapons 2, 5 and 7: his flinch, and his drop to one knee below 20000 life. `--life 30` brings out his overhead blow and his kill by the neck, which drags the player. Room 20C drops him from the ceiling when the player walks in under it: `--warp 20C:-10500:0:-14200:0 --flag 1:1 --flag 4:78`. With no input, the drop, the fight and the death match. With `mrx_shots.txt` and weapon 7, his charge and the kill by the neck match too. Rooms 109 (`--warp 109:-4696:0:-1380:560 --flag 1:6`) and 10C (`--warp 10C:-25450:-3600:-15800:0:7 --flag 1:6`) match to the game over. Rooms 117, 507, 602, 604 (both characters), 612 and 30A, entered through their doors with the flags their scripts check, match while he waits there for the script; give Claire's rooms `--player 1`.

The plants (`Em_2E` for ids 0x2e and 0x39, `Em_2F` for the vines, id 0x2f) are in `zm` too. Room 110 places two poison ivies and a template vine they grow six vines from, under flag 1:6 only (in both scenarios and for both players: no other flag places them). Run `--warp 110:-13697:-7200:8942:3224:5 --flag 1:6 --life 200` with no input, against an arklay run of `--room 110` with a setup script that places the player, sets the life and the flag and calls `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`. Compare at `--offset 344`. The room's load draws `Rnd` 21 times: four for each ivy, two for each vine it grows and one for the template. Every plant, the player and `Rnd` match for the 1000 ticks run, through the first ivy's grab; the vines are 1 or 2 units out from tick 418. With the player at `-14300, -7200, 9900` it's the same. The vines' positions come from the ivy's posed parts, so they show any difference in its neck turn first. Room 205 places two ivies and a template under flag 1:6 as well: run `--warp 205:-12000:0:-12000:0 --flag 1:6 --life 200` with no input. Its first ivy starts in state 5, the spit from range, and the stale `Dest` its slot keeps from an earlier room turns its first walk ticks (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`). Room 207 places two 0x39 poison ivies and a template under the same flag: run `--warp 207:-20500:0:-13500:1024 --flag 1:6 --life 200` with no input. The original pushes the player to `z` -13012 before play begins, so arklay's setup script places the player there. The plants, the player and `Rnd` match to the end of the reference, through the first ivy's spit, its hit on the player at tick 259, the poison and its first life taken.

`plant_shots.txt` fights the plants of rooms 110 and 207 with a shot every 30 ticks: add `--equip W --give 0:W:200 --give 1:0x14:60` to the runs above, with 2300 ticks. Compare at `--offset 344` against arklay's `--room` run with the input moved 344 ticks earlier and the same setup script plus `api:give_item(W, 200)`, `api:give_item(0x14, 60)` and `api:set_equipped_weapon(W)`. With weapons 2, 5, 6, 7 and 8, the plants' routines, motion and life and the player's routine and life match through hits, the retreat, a hold (weapons 2 and 6 in room 110), the deaths, the parts thrown and the vines' fall, to the end of 1956 ticks. `Rnd` matches too, except with weapon 6 in room 110: a spent case lands at tick 425 there, the original draws `Rnd` once more on the next tick, and it is one draw out from then on. Positions are exact with weapons 5 and 7 in both rooms and with 6 and 8 in room 207, and otherwise within 7 to 16 units (32 for weapon 6 in room 110). The details are in `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`. With weapon 3 the reference crashes before play begins in both rooms.

The giant spider (`Em_25`, id 0x25, `enemy/25/enemy.lua`) is in `zm` too. Room 302 with flag 1:6 places two floor spiders: run `--warp 302:-6611:0:-3724:0 --flag 1:6 --life 200` with no input over 2400 ticks, against an arklay run of `--room 302` with a setup script that places the player at `-1801, 0, -3724` (the original pushes the player there before play begins), angle 0, sets health 200 and flag 1:6 and calls `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, over 2056 ticks. Compare at `--offset 344` with `--fields rng,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,zm0,zm1`. The room's load draws `Rnd` twice for each spider, and `Sca_ck_em` pushes the first 600 units out of a wall. With the player that far away the spiders only rest, walk and turn, and `Rnd`, the player and both spiders match for all 2056 ticks. Without the flag the room places a wall spider (`Type` 9) and a floor spider instead (below).

The floor spiders' attack states want the player within 8000 units. Run `--warp 302:-13000:0:-12000:0 --flag 1:6 --life 200` with no input over 1400 ticks, and arklay's `--room 302` with a setup script that places the player at `-13000, 0, -12000` (the original leaves the player there), angle 0, sets health 200 and flag 1:6 and calls `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, over 1056 ticks. Compare at `--offset 344` with the same fields. The spiders, about 5300 units from the player, turn toward the player (`Routine_1` 5), lunge (7) and spit (8), and `Rnd`, the player and both spiders match for all 1056 ticks. With the player at `-13500, 0, -8500` (both sides), the second spider starts about 2400 units away. Its lunges stop where the bodies' pushes leave it, about 1770 units from the player, outside the bite's 1500, and that run matches for all 1056 ticks too. The noise wants a shot: run `--warp 302:-6611:0:-3724:0 --flag 1:6 --life 200 --give 0:2:90 --give 1:0x14:60 --equip 2` with `oracle/inputs-re2/spider_noise.txt` (a shot every 20 ticks from 420 to 800, facing away from the spiders), and arklay as in the first recipe, also calling `api:give_item(2, 90)`, `api:give_item(0x14, 60)` and `api:set_equipped_weapon(2)`, with the input 344 ticks earlier. A spider resting when a shot goes off leaps aside (9) or turns (5), and all 1056 ticks match. Between them the three runs enter state 5 13 times, 7 32 times, 8 7 times and 9 8 times. Room 302's `aot_set 2` is a water area (kind 7, `Sce_Water`, level -180) over the whole of `x` -20592 to -2592 and `z` -20566 to 8804, so a walk there takes `Snd_se_walk`'s `Water` path, whose three ripples draw `Rnd` once each on every footfall (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`). `water_walk.txt` holds `up` from tick 360: run `--warp 302:-13500:0:-8500:2048 --flag 1:6 --life 200` with it over 1400 ticks, and arklay's `--room 302` with a setup script that calls `api:set_player_pos(-13500, 0, -8500)`, `api:set_player_angle(2048)`, `api:set_health(200)`, `api:set_re2_flag(1, 6)` and `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, with the input 344 ticks earlier (`16 up`), over 1056 ticks. The original's rows from 38 on are room 302's, and play settles at 344 as in the other recipes, so compare at `--offset 344` with the same fields. The player walks on the spot there in both games, and the first footfall is at tick 22, where arklay used to part from the original. `Rnd`, the player and both spiders match for all 1056 ticks. The same with angle 0 on both sides (`--warp 302:-13500:0:-8500:0`, `api:set_player_angle(0)`) matches for all 1056 ticks too. `spider_shots.txt` shoots the floor spiders: it aims from tick 346 and fires every 30 ticks from 360 to 1320. In the commands below, `W` is the weapon's id. Run `--warp 302:-13000:0:-12000:0 --flag 1:6 --life 200 --give 0:W:200 --give 1:0x14:60 --equip W` with it over 1400 ticks, and arklay as in the first recipe, also calling `api:give_item(W, 200)`, `api:give_item(0x14, 60)` and `api:set_equipped_weapon(W)`, with the input 344 ticks earlier, over 1056 ticks. Compare at `--offset 344` with the same fields. The player turns to the nearest spider at each aim. With weapons 2, 5, 6 and 8, `Rnd`, the player and both spiders match for all 1056 ticks, through hits (`Routine_0` 2) and the leaps and turns that follow them, both deaths (3), a handgun's body shot again, and the bodies (7). With weapon 2 the same input and place, but aiming from 380 and firing from 400 (spider 1 is then lunging at the player, who is pushed out of it), matches for all 1056 ticks too. With weapon 7, `Rnd`, the routines, frames and life match to the end, but the player's `z` is one unit out from tick 9, after a push out of a spider, and positions differ by up to 12 units after that. In this water every aim and shot splashes (`Pl_water`, twelve draws of `Rnd` on each odd frame up to 5) and every spent case ends in a splash at the water's level (`Esp_05`'s `Water_ck`), so these runs check both.

The spiders on walls and ceilings are checked in rooms 302 (without the flag), 407 and 405, all with `--life 200`, against arklay runs of the room with a setup script that places the player, sets health 200 and calls `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, over 2056 ticks, compared at `--offset 344` with the fields above. Room 302 without the flag places a wall spider (`Type` 9, slot 0) and a floor spider (slot 1): with `--warp 302:-6611:0:-3724:0` and no input (the original pushes the player to `-1801, 0, -3724` before play begins, as in the first recipe) both only wait, and all 2056 ticks match. Room 407 places two ceiling spiders (`Type` 0x43): run `--warp 407:-2700:0:-19832:2028` with no input, and the player at `-2700, 0, -19832`, angle 2028. All 2056 ticks match, through the ceiling's rest, walk, turn and lunge with its spits (7), the way down a wall (10), the way round the other spider (11), and on the wall the way down, the wait, the crawl, the lunge, the climb and the way back onto the ceiling; the spiders' bites take the player from 200 life to 70. Room 405 places a wall spider (`Type` 0x4b, slot 0) and a ceiling spider (`Type` 0x43, slot 1): run `--warp 405:-26394:0:-1920:97` with no input, and the player at `-26394, 0, -1920`, angle 97. `Rnd`, the player's place and both spiders match for all 2056 ticks, but from tick 532 the original's player takes 30 life that arklay's doesn't: a ceiling lunge's spit lands on them. With the blob's behaviours and room 405's own stream (`Esp_47`, `Esp_44`, `Esp_55`) ported, the trace and the effects match the original for all 2056 ticks (the run `spider_405`). The shots use `spider_shots.txt` with `--give 0:W:200 --give 1:0x14:60 --equip W`, and the same items, `api:set_equipped_weapon(W)` and the input moved 344 ticks earlier on arklay's side. In room 302 with weapon 2 and the player at `-12500, 0, -13000`, angle 2048 (`--warp 302:-12500:0:-13000:2048`), all 1056 ticks match, effects included: the wall spider's spit (7), two hits (the second on its way up), its climbs, the way onto the ceiling, the ceiling's rest, walk, turn, leap and lunge, the way back down a wall and the crawl and lunge there. At tick 820 that lunge's spit lands on the player for 30 life, and the blobs squash and splash where they land (`Esp_36` to `Esp_39`, `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`). This is the regression run `spider_302_wall`, and `spider_407` is room 407's recipe above; both are in `oracle/re2-regress.d/spider.toml`, recorded with sound and every frame. In room 405 (the place above) with weapon 2, the player's `x` and `z` are a unit out from tick 8, where aiming pushes the player off a wall (as on main, and not the spiders' doing). Even so, `Rnd`, the player's life and the wall spider match for all 2056 ticks, through four hits on a wall, its climbs onto the ceiling and states 10, 11 and 7 there; the ceiling spider matches until tick 774, where it follows the player's unit. With weapon 5 `Rnd`, the player's life and both spiders match for all 2056 ticks: both spiders die on a wall (`0x4722f0`), fall (from `Move_cnt` 9 of clip 1, past its end), lie on the floor and twitch. With weapon 8 the hit on the wall spider at tick 19 takes a leg off, and the same fields match until tick 205, where the ceiling spit's 30 life lands on the player. The shotgun's fire clip takes the hand (part 14) under room 405's deep water (-1040), so its splash there depends on reading the parts as the last tick posed them (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`). To reach the ceiling spider, `spider_up_shots.txt` aims up from the same place, with the same items. With weapon 8 `Rnd`, the player's life and both spiders match for all 2056 ticks, through a hit on the ceiling (tick 79) and a death there (137). With weapon 5 they parted at tick 48, before room 405's stream (behaviour 0x47) was ported, which kept more effect slots busy in the original; that run hasn't been compared since. With weapon 2 they part at tick 139, after a hit on the ceiling at tick 107; that wasn't traced further. In all of these the player's `x` and `z` are a unit out from tick 8. Rooms 407 and 302 at `-1801, 0, -3724` push the aiming player off a wall too, so their shot runs part at tick 8. Room D05 can't be reached this way: `--warp D05:...` lands in room 405.

The young spider (`Em_26`, id 0x26, `enemy/26/enemy.lua`) is in `zm` too. Of the rooms that place young, only room 201 can be reached by `--warp`: the letter rooms (B09, C07, C08, F0A, F10, F13 and F15) land in the numbered room (`--warp C07:...` in 307, `--warp B09:...` in 209). Room 201 with flag 1:6 places a young template (slot 0, `Type` 0, which becomes `0x4000`) and two floor spiders (slots 1 and 2). Run `--warp 201:-19000:0:-23000:0 --flag 1:6 --life 200` with no input over 1400 ticks, against an arklay run of `--room 201 --character 0` with a setup script that places the player at `-19000, 0, -23000`, angle 0, sets health 200 and flag 1:6 and calls `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, over 1056 ticks. Compare at `--offset 344` with `--fields rng,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,zm0,zm1` (and `zm2` to `zm8` for the young). All 1056 ticks match. To send young, run the same with `spider_shots.txt` and `--give 0:5:200 --give 1:0x14:60 --equip 5`, and the same items, `api:set_equipped_weapon(5)` and the input moved 344 ticks earlier on arklay's side. The first spider's death at tick 18 sends six young into slots 3 to 8, and they match until tick 77, where a young's turn toward the player is a unit out. That unit is the player's: every aim in room 201 puts the player a unit or two from the original at tick 8, with weapons 2, 5 and 7 and on main too. Facing the aim's target (angle 2437) they part at tick 50; from `-17836, 0, -19643`, angle 2048, at tick 75 with weapon 5 and 72 with weapon 7.

The G embryos (`Em_27`, id 0x27, `enemy/27/enemy.lua`) are in `zm` too. Rooms 303 (Leon, `main_0B`, which needs flag 4:55) and 309 (Claire, `main_05`) in scenario A place the template in slot 1 and, with flag 4:57 set (the room's first-entry event done), two free embryos in slots 2 and 3 while the G adult (`Em_28`, slot 0) is alive; without 4:57 there are none. the records that don't follow the G adult keep only slots 1 to 3 (`--no-enemies --keep-enemy 1 --keep-enemy 2 --keep-enemy 3`). Its init still draws one `Rnd` in the load before the slot goes, and arklay's does too: the setup scripts start `Rnd` at 6114 and switch the slot off with `set_re2_no_enemies`, and the adult's init and the embryos' take it to 58231. The runs are in `oracle/re2-regress.d/em27.toml`, with no input, compared at `--offset 344` with `--fields room,cam,rng,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,zm1,zm2,zm3`; the setup scripts (`oracle/inputs-re2/embryo_*.lua`) also give the speeds and the facing history the new works' memory holds (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "The G embryo"), which are the same for both of room 303's doors:

- `embryo_wait_303` (`--warp 303:-24972:-5400:-13739:552:3 --flag 4:55 --flag 4:57`, from room 304's door): the embryos crawl, slide and rest, and embryo 3 jumps at the player and takes the latch (`Routine_1` 4) at tick 737: it climbs and winds round the player, holds on (the player's `p.beh` 5 from tick 855), climbs to the head and bites (200 life to 190), jumps off and falls back to the crawl, then latches on again. All 1056 ticks match, the player's 140 life at the end included.
- `embryo_jump_303` (`--warp 303:-24700:-5400:-25832:3808:0`, the same flags, from room 30B's door): embryo 3 faces the player within 3000 and jumps on its first move. All 56 ticks match.
- `embryo_wait_309` (`--start claire --warp 309:-17273:-5400:1678:488:3 --flag 4:57`, from room 30A's door): as in 303, and both embryos latch on at once from tick 875; one's jump off cuts the other's bite short (`fg_room_enemy`'s bit 0 sends it to `Routine_2` 9). All 1056 ticks match, the player's 110 life at the end included.
- `embryo_struggle_303` (`embryo_wait_303`'s start with confirm pressed every fourth tick from 740, no sound): the presses shake the latching embryo off, and all 1056 ticks match. `embryo_kill_303` (the same start with `--life 10`, no input): the first bite kills and the embryo goes in (`Routine_2` 11 to 13); the bite holds back `Die` as a dog's does, and 881 of 1056 ticks match. At reference tick 1225 the original freezes the world a tick before arklay does (the player's routine 5, 0, 8 and what ends it are unported), the run's `expect`. `g_adult_wait_303` (Leon A from room 304's door with the G adult kept, standing, 2400 ticks; `zm` lists id 0x28 since #690, so the reference is `g_adult_wait_303_zm0`, recorded on the VPS) has the adult (`Em_28`, slot 0, `Type` 1) walk toward the player, turn and bite, and spit the embryos (#690): the adult matches the original on 1189 of its ticks, and every `Rnd` draw to tick 1046. The first difference is tick 353, where the embryo that rides out of the adult's mouth starts a few units from the original's: the adult turns parts 2 to 4 toward the player (`Rot_neck_em_n`), which isn't ported, and an embryo's place is read from part 4.

The cockroaches (`Em_29`, id 0x29, `enemy/29/enemy.lua`) are in `zm` too, in up to 24 slots: the tracer reads every slot `Game_loop` runs, and `--no-enemies` and `--keep-enemy` reach all of them. Room 40B's `init_00` sets 24 at the top of its pipe while the partner flag (1:3) is clear. Entered from room 405's door, `--warp 40B:-7824:0:-25140:2000:0 --life 200` with no input over 1400 ticks, against an arklay run of `--room 40B` with the setup script `swarm_stand_40b.lua` (the player at `-7824, 0, -25140`, angle 2000, health 200, `Rnd` 6114, camera 0, and `api:set_re2_neck_wait(41)`: the original's `Pl_neck` first looks on tick 386), the room's load draws `Rnd` 24 times, one for each roach's facing. Compare at `--offset 344` with `--fields room,cam,rng,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,zm*`. `rng`, the player and all 24 roaches match to the end: their waits, crawls, jumps and flights, and from tick 438, when slot 1 lands on its point on the player's part 1, the landings, the player's struggle (`p.beh` 5), the falls and the climbs back onto the pipe. A roach in flight steers at a point on one of the player's parts, and one on the player is traced at its place in the world (the draw's `Joint_trans2` writes it into `Pos`), so the roaches show any difference in the player's pose or head turn first; compare `neck` and `pneck` to find it. The regression runner's `swarm_stand_40b` replays it. A record warped to `-6750, 0, -25200`, past the roaches' limit at the pipe's 405 end, exits the original.

The alligator (`Em_23`, id 0x23, `enemy/23/enemy.lua`) is in `zm` too. Room 40A places it while flag 4:51 is clear, and it waits under the water until the room's scene starts it. Entered from room 408's door, `--warp 40A:-9093:-1800:-857:2064:8 --life 200` with no input over 1400 ticks, against an arklay run of `--room 40A` with the player at `-9093, -1800, -857`, angle 2064, health 200, `Rnd` 6114 and camera 8, it only waits, and all 1056 ticks match at `--offset 344` on `room,cam,rng`, the player and `zm0`. In the scene's area, `--warp 40A:2000:-1800:-23000:1128:10 --life 200` with no input over 2400 ticks, against `--room 40A` with the player at `2000, -1800, -23000`, angle 1128 and camera 10, the scene runs (the player's walks, the camera's cuts, the splashes), the alligator enters and rests, and it swims up the corridor after the player; the camera, `Rnd`, the player and `zm0` match on all 1250 ticks, through its boxes' push, four snaps that knock the player on from behind (from tick 883) and the player's death. The scene's splashes draw 44 `Rnd` in each round, as the original's do (`Esp_tbl`'s 0x52, 0x4A and 0x15, `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`). Both games go to the game over at tick 1594. With `alligator_face.txt` (right from tick 820 to 852, shifted by 344), the player turns to face it after the scene and is thrown back from the front (player routine 5,2) at ticks 539, 624, 705 and 788 of arklay's run, and all 1261 ticks to the game over at tick 1605 match. With `alligator_tank.txt` (after the scene, run along the corridor and up its second leg to the button at `aot` 2, `x` -22241 and `z` -8591, turn to face it and press, then turn to the alligator, aim from tick 1346 and fire every 30 ticks from 1360), the tank falls (ticks 978 to 1034), the alligator bites it at tick 1334 and holds it from 1372, and shrugs off the shot at 1361; the shot at 1391 sets the tank off, and the alligator is thrown back dead and burns. All 2056 ticks match on `room,cam,rng`, the player and `zm0`. With `alligator_end.txt` (after the scene, run along the corridor and up its second leg to its end, and wait), the player is knocked on three times, the alligator rests at the end from tick 1694 and takes the player under at 1698, and both games go to the game over at tick 2146; all 1802 ticks match. With `alligator_dive.txt` and the magnum (`--equip 5 --give 0:5:200`, and the same in arklay's setup script `alligator_dive_40a.lua`), the player turns round at the end of the first leg and fires every 20 ticks (the magnum fires every 40) as the alligator swims up the second leg, through three knocks; the hits take it to 24990 before it rests at the corridor's end, and the hit at tick 1681, in cut 8, makes it dive. It waits for the player to lower the magnum, goes under in cut 9 from tick 1718, and is gone in cut 8 at 2013. All 2056 ticks match. With `alligator_drag.txt` (after the scene, run along the corridor and stop just inside its second leg at tick 900), the alligator's turn into the second leg brings its body within reach of the player, who is caught at tick 996 (`FUN_00469750`, player routine 5,1) and dragged under, with no knock first; both games go to the game over at tick 1380, and all 1036 ticks match. With the same input in scenario B with zapping (`--flag 1:1 --flag 29:16`, character 2, and the same flags in arklay's setup script `alligator_zap_40a.lua`), the room uses cameras 13 and 14 where scenario A uses 6 and 7, and the hit at tick 1681 makes it take the zapping dive (`FUN_0046a430`) in cut 14 from tick 1718, easing to `z` 0 by 1763 and settling at 1815; all 2056 ticks match. With `alligator_grenade.txt` and the grenade launcher (`--equip-load 9 --give 0:9:30`, which holds the launcher before the warp's door so the room loads its files, and the same in arklay's setup script `alligator_grenade_40a.lua`), the player turns to face it after the scene, aims from tick 854 and fires every 40 ticks from 900 (shifted by 344): the grenades hit it for 30 a time at ticks 901, 941, 983, 1021 and 1065, and it bleeds from the head (`FUN_0046b540`, effect 5 row 4) and carries on; all 1356 ticks match on `room,cam,rng`, the player and `zm0`. The player's `Spd_y` reads -1 in the original's walk where arklay's is 0 (from tick 517), so this run leaves out `mot`, as the other runs in this room do. The regression runner's `alligator_wait_40a`, `alligator_scene_40a`, `alligator_face_40a`, `alligator_tank_40a`, `alligator_end_40a`, `alligator_dive_40a`, `alligator_drag_40a`, `alligator_zap_40a` and `alligator_grenade_40a` replay them.

G1 (`Em_30`, id 0x30, `enemy/30/enemy.lua`) is in `zm` too. Room 303 places it in scenario B while flag 4:55 is set, and it waits there until the room's scene starts it, which the room's event 17 does when the player enters cut 2. Entered from room 30B's door, `--warp 303:-24972:-5400:-13739:552:3 --flag 1:1 --flag 4:55 --life 200` with no input over 1400 ticks, against an arklay run of `--room 303 --character 2` with the player at `-24972, -5400, -13739`, angle 552, health 200, `Rnd` 6114, camera 5 and flags 1:1 and 4:55, it only waits, and all 1056 ticks match at `--offset 344` on `room,cam,rng`, the player and `zm0`. To start the scene, `--warp 303:-16852:-5400:-19090:552:3` with the same flags and `g1_scene_303.txt` (`up` from tick 344 to 380) walks the player into cut 2: over 2400 ticks, against arklay with the player at `-16852, -5400, -19090` and camera 3 and the input 344 ticks earlier, the scene (the player's walks, the camera's cuts, G1 placed by the script) and G1's change its walk after the player, a lunge that strikes a wall, the pull to free its arm and its charge, which kills the player, match on `rng`, the player and `zm0` to the game over at tick 2311. Warped straight into cut 2, the original's scene starts a tick before arklay's, so the run walks in. With `g1_flee_303.txt` (the same, then `right` from tick 1128 and `up` with `run` from 1144 to 1744), the player runs east once the scene is over, and G1's walk across the room, its lunges, a wall's strike, its rearing up and the charge that kills the player match on all 2056 ticks. With `g1_hit_303.txt` (`up` from 1128 to 1144) the player walks into a lunge, falls back onto a wall (`pl_dm02`), gets up and falls forwards from a second hit (`pl_dm03`), and everything matches through G1's charge and the kill to the game over at tick 2087; with `g1_hit_side_303.txt` (`right` from 1128, `up` from 1136 to 1184) a lunge from the side throws the player forwards, G1's step in throws them again, and it matches through the charge and the kill to the game over at tick 2085. The runs end at the game over, where the original returns to the title. To shoot G1, give the player Leon's weapons for room 303 in scenario B, the handgun, the shotgun and the magnum, with the one fired equipped and a large magazine to avoid a reload: for example `--equip 5 --give 0:5:200 --give 1:2:15 --give 2:7:5 --give 3:0x16:30`, and arklay's setup script places the same items with `api:set_item_slot` and `api:set_equipped`. `g1_magnum_303.txt` walks in for the scene as `g1_scene_303.txt` does, turns right from tick 1128 to 1132 to face G1, aims from 1134 and fires every 20 ticks from 1150. G1 flinches, dies at tick 1358, gets up and hits the player, limps away and falls, and all 2456 ticks match. With the shotgun every 30 ticks (`g1_shotgun_303.txt`) or the handgun every 15 (`g1_handgun_303.txt`, which gives the magnum in slot 1 and the shotgun in slot 2: with the shotgun in slot 1 the original exits at boot), G1 kills the player first, and both match to the game over at ticks 2315 and 2230. The regression runner's `g1_wait_303`, `g1_scene_303`, `g1_flee_303`, `g1_hit_303`, `g1_hit_side_303`, `g1_shotgun_303`, `g1_magnum_303` and `g1_handgun_303` replay them.

G2 (`Em_31`, id 0x31, `enemy/31/enemy.lua`) is in `zm` too. Room 509 places it in scenario A, off the stage at `32000, 0, 32000`, where it waits for the room's scene. Off the lift from room 505, `--warp 509:13248:-1800:-16378:3480:5 --flag 4:99 --life 200` with no input over 1400 ticks (room 505's lift goes to room 509 while flag 4:99 is set and 7:23 clear), against an arklay run of `--room 509 --character 0` with the player at `13248, -1800, -16378`, angle 3480, health 200, `Rnd` 6114, camera 5 and flag 4:99, it only waits, and all 1056 ticks match at `--offset 344` on `room,cam,rng`, the player and `zm0`. The regression runner's `g2_wait_509` replays it.

Room 509's scene starts when the player enters `aot` 1 (`7505, -13822`, 1850 by 8900) on floor 0. The lift arrives on floor 1, and no walk from its cage down to floor 0 was found, so the run warps onto floor 0 inside the area: `--warp 509:8430:0:-10000:2048:2 --flag 4:99 --life 200` with no input over 981 ticks. The scene runs from tick 382, G2 changes from tick 444, climbs down and leaves routine 7 for the fight at tick 980. Against an arklay run of `--room 509 --character 0` with the player at `8430, 0, -10000`, angle 2048, health 200, `Rnd` 6114, camera 2 and flag 4:99, all 637 ticks match at `--offset 344` on `room,cam,rng`, the player and `zm0`. The regression runner's `g2_scene_509` replays it. Past tick 981 G2 fights, and with 200 life a 3000-tick record fails as the game exits at the player's death. With `--life 2000` instead (health 2000 in the setup), G2's walks, swipes, back steps, grabs and throws run for the whole 3000 ticks and leave the player on 1186; all 2656 ticks match on the same fields. The regression runner's `g2_fight_509` replays it. With 200 life and no input (`g2_kill_509`), the player falls near death by tick 1900, draws G2's dodges, and is caught by a grab and killed at tick 2196; the game over ends at tick 2719, and all 2376 ticks match. With the shotgun (`--equip 7 --give 0:7:200 --give 1:2:15 --give 2:5:6 --give 3:0x15:30`, `g2_shotgun_509.lua` on arklay's side, and `g2_shotgun_509.txt`: right from tick 912 to 938 to face G2, aim from 940 and fire every 30 ticks from 970), the hits take G2 to 81 life, it mutates at tick 1932, and dies at tick 2141; with the magnum (`--equip 5 --give 0:5:200 --give 1:2:15 --give 2:7:5 --give 3:0x16:30`, `g2_magnum_509.txt`, every 20 ticks from 960) it mutates at tick 1575 and dies at 1701. Both run 3000 ticks on 2000 life, and all 2656 ticks of each match. The regression runner's `g2_shotgun_509` and `g2_magnum_509` replay them.

G3 (`Em_33`, id 0x33, `enemy/33/enemy.lua`) is in `zm` too. Room 509 places it in scenario B (flag 1:1), off the stage at `32000, 0, 32000`, where it waits for the room's scene. Off the lift from room 505, `--warp 509:13248:-1800:-16378:3480:5 --flag 1:1 --flag 4:99 --life 200` with no input over 1400 ticks, against an arklay run of `--room 509 --character 0` with the player at `13248, -1800, -16378`, angle 3480, health 200, `Rnd` 6114, camera 5 and flags 1:1 and 4:99, it only waits, drawing one or two `Rnd`s a tick for its eye, and all 1056 ticks match at `--offset 344` on `room,cam,rng`, the player and `zm0`. The regression runner's `g3_wait_509` replays it. Room 509's scene for G3 starts when the player enters `aot` 2 (`-6595, -15122`, 5850 by 1900) on floor 0: `--warp 509:-3670:0:-14172:2048:0 --flag 1:1 --flag 4:99 --life 2000` with no input over 2000 ticks. The scene runs from tick 345 (the script first walks G3 in at `439, 0, -23018`, then places it at `-3600, -500, -21420` at tick 465), it rises from tick 485 and starts the fight at tick 778. Against an arklay run with the player at `-3670, 0, -14172`, angle 2048, health 2000, `Rnd` 6114, camera 0 and flags 1:1 and 4:99, all 1656 ticks match on the same fields: the scene, G3's roar, walks, turns and swipes, and the player's knock-backs, which leave them on 1670. The regression runner's `g3_scene_509` replays it. The same start with no input over 5000 ticks (`g3_fight_509`) leaves the player on 710 after 42 knock-backs, and all 4656 ticks match. With 200 life (`--life 200`, `g3_kill_509`), the player falls below 20 by tick 1555, G3 lunges at them twice and kills them in its grip from tick 1811, and the game over starts at tick 2342, where the run ends; all 1998 ticks match. Two runs shoot it from the same start with 2000 life, `--give 1:2:15 --give 3:0x15:30` (or `0x16`) and the weapon in slot 0: the shotgun (`--equip 7 --give 0:7:200 --give 2:5:6`, `g3_shotgun_509`), turning right at 780 and aiming from 795, firing every 30 ticks from 825, and the magnum (`--equip 5 --give 0:5:200 --give 2:7:5`, `g3_magnum_509`), firing every 20 from 815. The shotgun's hits flinch G3 high (routine 8) and low (routine 9, into `+0x218`'s mode), its second swipes grip the player four times, and the hit at 2266 kills it (the death from 2326); the magnum's spurt runs on each hit, and it dies at 1216. Each matches on every field until G3 first grips the player (1244 and 1060), where the player comes out a unit off (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, G3), and G3's routines and life follow the original's through its death after that.

Room 205's ten bodies in the walls (`Em_2D`, id 0x2D, `enemy/2d/enemy.lua`) are in `zm` too, as `zm0` to `zm9`. The room places them in scenario A and freezes them (`Type`'s 0x8000, so `zm` prints 32784, 32769 and 32768) until the player enters one of two areas: event 4's (`-14500, -10100`, 3300 by 3400) releases slots 5 to 9 on the east wall and event 3's (`-27868, -13500`, 4000 by 3200) slots 0 to 4 on the west. `main_00` makes both areas inert unless flag 4:48 is set, which only room 10C sets, so every run warps with it, from the door from room 206: `--warp 205:-16798:0:-10671:3136:4 --flag 4:48 --life 200`. On arklay's side, `--room 205` with the player at `-16798, 0, -10671`, angle 3136, health 200, `Rnd` 6114, camera 4 and flag 4:48. With no input over 1400 ticks (`em2d_load_205`) only the load and the freeze show. `em2d_watch_205.txt` walks north and east into event 4's area and stands at `-12895, -9074`, about 2100 from the east wall, then walks west and down the west corridor along its east wall (`x` about -25300, about 1850 from the west wall) into event 3's area, and stands at three places in turn (`z` -10382, -11567 and -12209) so that slots 0 and 2, then 3, then 1 and 4 come out. Each body backs 400 into the wall, arms `Timer0 + 1` ticks later, comes out 50 a tick on clip 0, turns its head on clip 1 or 2 within 0x180 of home for 60 to 91 ticks, and goes back in on clip 4 to lie hidden at life -1. The corridors are narrow: a body's arm (part 3 or 10) reaches 900 from a player about 1900 from its wall, so the walk keeps out of reach. All 1156 ticks match at `--offset 344` on `room,cam,rng`, the player, `mot` and `zm0` to `zm9` (`em2d_watch_205`), and as Claire with `--start claire` (`em2d_watch_205_claire`). `em2d_grab_205.txt` takes the same walk into event 4's area, then turns left for 16 ticks and walks 16 at the east wall: on tick 476 slot 7's arm takes the player (`Routine_1` 4, the player in routine 5 at the arm) while the other four hold (6), and with no presses the grab runs its 150 ticks; then the four turn their heads again, reach, and, with `fg_room_enemy` at 120, go back in. `em2d_grab_205_mash.txt` presses confirm every fourth tick from 484 to 580, which ends the grab 48 ticks early; `em2d_grab_205_claire` plays it as Claire. `em2d_shots_205.txt` (with `--give 0:2:200 --give 1:0x14:60 --equip 2`, `em2d_shots_205.lua` on arklay's side) stands out of reach and fires four handgun shots 30 ticks apart while the bodies are out.

`--fields rng,zm0,zm1,zm2,zm3,zm4,zm5` compares `Rnd` and each zombie's position, angle, routine, motion, `Type` and life, from the trace's `zm` field. With no input, room 100 matches from t=1 to t=193, when one reaches Leon.

Rooms 610 and 11B place zombies that can see the player's start. Run `--warp 610:-19500:0:-27000:0` or `--warp 11B:8000:0:-19500:0` with no input, against arklay's `--room 610` or `--room 11B` with a setup script that places the player, sets health 200 and calls `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, and compare at `--offset 344`. Before play begins, the original pushes the player in room 610 to `-19771, 0, -26129`, so arklay's setup script places the player there: from `-19500, 0, -27000` the first zombie is beyond the stand's 5000 units. `Rnd`, the player and every zombie match over 1056 ticks in both rooms. The original's zombies that see the player (within 5000 units, facing it and in sight) leave the stand on the first tick. Under `--room` the player's head has no place of its own before the first tick, so the setup script puts it where the rest pose puts it at the new place, as the original's warp has it; without that, `Root_ck`'s sight check failed on ticks 0 and 1 and the zombies left the stand two ticks late. Neither room calls `sce_em_set2`, and the zombies match with `Dest` started at 0.

A real door into room 11B is room 11A's door 1, which puts the player at `-9500, 0, -20400`, angle 1024. Run `--warp 11A:9150:0:-2600:3072 --flag 6:156 --flag 6:157 --life 200` with the input `oracle/inputs-re2/door_11b.txt` (`344 up`, `354`, `364 action`, `368`) over 1200 ticks: the player stands against the door, and the action press at tick 364 opens it. The two flags are the dead flags of room 11A's zombies, which would otherwise reach the player first. On arklay's side, run `--room 11A` with a setup script that places the player there with angle 3072, sets health 200, calls `api:set_re2_flag(6, 156)`, `api:set_re2_flag(6, 157)` and `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, and the same input 344 ticks earlier, over 856 ticks. Compare at `--offset 344`. Room 11B's load comes at tick 399 and its zombies are placed at 400. Then play stays stopped until tick 704, the last stopped tick (`fg_stop` `0xEF000000`), and runs from 705. `Rnd`, the player, `mot` and all six zombies match to tick 1097, apart from the door's own tick (364), whose `rng` is known to differ. With `--flag 0:25` on the original and `api:set_re2_flag(0, 25)` in the setup script, the room places its other set of zombies, and those match over all 856 ticks too. `--fields pneck` compares where the player's head is (see the trace table). The original keeps room 11A's position, `9150, -2513, -2668`, from the door until tick 703, places the head in room 11B on tick 704, on the skeleton's rest pose (every joint's turn zeroed), and blends from it. arklay does the same, and the head matches on every tick. With the default set, zombie 2 used to leave its wander (routine 1,0,3) for the walk (1,1,1), drawing `Rnd`, on tick 1098 in the original and on tick 1099 in arklay. `Root_ck`'s turn (`+0x150`) and `Dest` (`+0x1c4`, `+0x1c6`) matched, and so did the player's neck. Zombie 2's own neck didn't: the original's joint `+0x5C` is the head on the body after `Oba_ck_em` and `Sca_ck_em` have pushed it, and every part's `+0x5C` and `+0x68` matches arklay's pose after the push. arklay took the head before the push. On tick 1097 the push moved it 18 and -14 units, and from there the original's line to the player was clear a tick earlier. arklay now places the neck after the pushes, and `Rnd`, the player, `mot` and all six zombies match over all 856 ticks, apart from the door's tick, with either set. A door with a standing zombie that faces its entry point within 5000 units, on the first tick of play, would show whether the gap matters. Room 60C's door into room 610 doesn't: its entry point, `-24424, 0, -14288`, is more than 5000 units from every zombie there.

The route's rooms 101, 11A and 11B have random walks entered through their doors: walks, turns, runs, backing and stops, written by a seeded generator. `walk_101.txt` is `door_101_102.txt`'s start through room 100's door and Kendo's scene, then from tick 2480 a walk through room 101 to tick 4400, with room 101's zombies on. Record it as `door_101_102.txt` is, `--ticks 4400`, and run arklay as there with `walk_101_arklay.txt` (the same input 780 ticks earlier) over 3620 ticks, comparing at `--offset 780` with `--fields room,cam,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,mot,rng,rseed,em,zm`. Everything matches to the end but the door's tick (790) and the start's `mot`, as in `door_101_102.txt`. `walk_11a.txt` opens room 119's door with `door_11b.txt`'s presses from `--warp 119:13032:0:-19315:0 --life 200` and walks through room 11A from tick 720, and `walk_11b.txt` does the same from `door_11b.txt`'s start in room 11A through room 11B. Run each over 2700 ticks, and arklay as the doors above, `--room 119` or `--room 11A` with the player where the original's tick 344 has them (`12921, 0, -20194`, angle 0, for room 119), health 200, `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)` and the input 344 ticks earlier, over 2356 ticks; compare at `--offset 344` with `--fields room,cam,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,mot,rng,zm`. `walk_11b.txt` wants `--flag 6:156 --flag 6:157` as `door_11b.txt` does. Both match on every tick but the door's (364): `walk_11a.txt` with room 11A's two zombies on, through their grabs and hits, and with them dead (`--flag 6:156 --flag 6:157`); `walk_11b.txt` with room 11B's zombies on, through their grabs to the game over, where both return to the title on tick 2227, and with them dead (`--flag 6:151` to `6:155` and `6:163`, plus room 11A's two), over all 2356 ticks.

`run_118.txt`, `run_119.txt`, `run_11a.txt`, `run_11b.txt`, `run_103.txt` and `run_200.txt` open each room's door from the previous room with `door_11b.txt`'s presses and then run a run-heavy random walk from tick 720 (runs in every direction, diagonals, backing, stops and an action press while running). Record each with the previous room's warp from the paragraph above: `--warp 102:-25067:0:15229:2048` for `run_118.txt`, `118:-24617:0:-24996:1024`, `119:13032:0:-19315:0`, `11A:9150:0:-2600:3072` with `--flag 6:156 --flag 6:157`, `11B:11393:0:-15351:3072` and `103:10303:-5400:-7823:0`, with `--life 200` over 2700 ticks, and run arklay with the previous room as `--room`, the player where the original's tick 344 has them, health 200, the flags, `api:set_re2_rnd(6114)`, the input 344 ticks earlier and `--ticks 2356`. Compare at `--offset 344` with `--fields room,cam,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,mot,rng,zm0,zm1,zm2,zm3,zm4,zm5`. Every field matches on every tick but `rng` on the door's tick, 364; in `run_119.txt` that takes in a zombie's hit from aligned tick 757 and the game over that follows (see `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "Player"). `grenade_118.txt` is `run_119.txt`'s start with the grenade launcher and grenade rounds: record it with `--warp 118:-24617:0:-24996:1024 --life 200 --equip-load 9 --give 0:9:30` over 1600 ticks and set up arklay with `grenade_118.lua`; it aims at the zombies from tick 360 and fires every 40 ticks, kills zombie 2 at tick 508 (200 a grenade) and zombie 1 at 702, and all 1256 ticks match on the fields above (the regression runner's `grenade_118`). `bowgun_118.txt` is the same start as Claire with the bowgun, which Leon can't hold. Record it with `--start claire --warp 118:-24617:0:-24996:1024 --life 200 --give 2:0xC:60` over 1800 ticks, and run arklay with `character = 1` and `player = 1` (without `player`, arklay's room loads Leon) and `bowgun_118.lua`, which gives Claire her own handgun (13), the knife and the bowgun in slot 2. `--equip-load 0xC` crashes the original at tick 38, as its bolts aren't set up, so the input EQUIPs the bowgun from the status screen instead, opening it at tick 346, before a zombie can grab her. It aims at 620 and fires every 40 ticks from 640: zombie 2 is knocked back at 646 and killed at 688, and zombie 1, three bolts at once, at 1003.
