It is one counter for every key (a new press of another key restarts it) and it keeps its value while nothing is held, except at 0, where the flag rises on the tick after the release too. `Status` sets (0xF01C, 0x60A) on every start (the status screen, the item box and each get-item window, on their sixth tick) and nothing sets it back, so play after the first screen or pick-up runs on it too; the title leaves (0x5000, 0x20A) (the flag on the press, +11, then every 3). The aim key (0x8) is in the status screen's mask.

The screens read "the flag and the key held" (`0x9885FC`) for the four directions (the item grid, the command list, the tab bar, COMBN's target, MAP's floors, FILE's carousel, list and pages, the pick-up's pages, the box's columns and rows) and the edge for decide, cancel and START. Three tests read the raw pad with no flag: FILE's carousel turn ends on a held left or right and starts the next turn at once (a turn is 13 ticks), the box's scroll goes on one row and one cue every 7 ticks while up or down is held, and the box's EXIT goes back to the inventory on a held down. `Pad_set` is at the top of `Psx_main`, so it runs on every frame, a door's included. arklay's `PadRepeat` (`src/game/pad_repeat.rs`) is stepped once a tick, a door's ticks included, before the screen's start and gives the screens their gated `Input`; the trace's opt-in `prep` field is the flag, the counter, the mask and the delays on both sides.

A recording's start leaves the counter at 8 in all three runs (the title's phase; `api:set_re2_pad_repeat(8)` in their setup scripts). The item page reads the pad from the screen's seventh tick, under the fade in: `Status` calls its page (`St_tbl`) on every tick after its start, so the page's description, messages and close run under the fade too. Runs, in `oracle/re2-regress.d/status_repeat.toml`, all matching on every compared field (`prep` included) on every tick:

- `status_repeat_102` (the inventory runs' warp with a herb pair, ammo and a blue herb in six slots): up held from 344 to 400, through START at 380 (the counter carries over from play, `prep` switches to 0xF01C/0x60A on the open's sixth tick and the first gated up comes on 388), the grid with right held 40 ticks and down held 20, the command list with down held, the tab bar, START, and up held in play again. COMBN's target isn't held through.
- `status_repeat_file` (files 1 and 3 in binder 0 and file 4 in binder 1): left held through three turns of the carousel, binder 0's list with down held, file 1 read with right held to its end marker, then left held back.
- `box_repeat_201` (`box_201`'s record): the box's rows and its inventory column with down, up and right held, up onto EXIT, and down and up held together.

- `file_pickup_repeat_201` (`file_pickup_201`'s record): the pick-up's pages (`St_get_file`) read the same gated input. Right held from 404 to 440 turns the memo's pages at 405 and then every 7 ticks to the end marker, left held from 480 and right from 520 do the same, and `prep` shows `Status`'s setting (0xF01C, 0x60A) from the window's sixth tick, 357.

## Room 115's scene

On Leon A's first visit, pressing the desk in room 115 (area 1, event 2) runs `main_02`, which gives the diary and the medal, then the scene with Claire (`main_03`): `fg_status`'s 0x10 (`scene`) is up from the first of its steps to the last. The runs raise 8:20 and 8:104 (`--flag 8:20 --flag 8:104`, which the recorder writes when play begins, before the room's `init_00`), so `Item_aot_set` sets both items' areas to kind 0 and the press goes straight from cut 9 through the two sleeps to the scene, with no window. The enemies stay on, since Claire is the room's own (`sce_em_set` slot 0, id 0x51). The player stands at (-20000, 0, -18100) facing 3072, which is the desk's press point; the press is at 350, `scene_115.txt`'s only key.

Each run is recorded with `--sound` and every frame, which runs at about 3.6 ticks a second, so DirectSound's clock ends each voice line (xa 22 to 28, and xa 29 in event 8) after a different number of ticks than at 30 a second: the runs set `voice_from_ref`, and the runner passes arklay each line's length from the reference's `fgstat` ("Sound"). `fgstat` is not compared; `xavol` is.

- `scene_115` (1880 ticks): in the reference the scene (cut 9 on 351, cut 5 on 423, `scene` up from 426 to 1680) plays the seven lines (`fgstat`'s 0x20 up 472 to 479, 565 to 575, 676 to 694, 1090 to 1105, 1364 to 1368, 1408 to 1413 and 1567 to 1576), the nine subtitles, Claire's moves and the door sounds, and hands the player back at 1680 at (-23037, 0, -19397), angle 2107, camera 2, with Claire standing at (-24574, 0, -19632). The run goes on 200 ticks past it.
- `scene_115_exit` (2100 ticks): from 1700 the input turns right 13 ticks (to 3067), walks north from 1715 (camera 1 on 1769, which `main_05` answers by putting Claire at (-20513, 0, -18890) on 1770), then south from 1779 (camera 2 on 1795, which starts event 6 on 1796): Claire walks to (-18730, 0, -18337), within 100 of her point (the walk ends on 1823), turns from 1824 to 3415 (five ticks at 96, within 350 of facing (-18785, -12553)) and starts clip 22 on 1830.
- `scene_115_line` (2000 ticks): from 1700 the player walks west for 16 ticks to (-23989, 0, -19087) and presses (1730), which is event 8 on area 26: xa 29 with message 9, `scene` up again from 1731 to 1819, with the line's bit held from 1731 to 1741.

The runs match on every compared field (`room,cam,rng,rseed,p.*,mot,pneck,inv,snd,bgm,xavol,em,msg,scene`) on every tick, with no change to Claire's walk or turn values: `plc_dest` for id 0x51 and the arrival flags that last one pass of the scripts are the original's. The props `super_set` hangs on the hands (objects 8 and 9, from `main_16` to `main_04`: object 9 on Leon's joint 1, object 8 on his joint 14 and then on Claire's, enemy slot 0's joint 14) are drawn on the joints of the pose the frame draws, where the original draws them: `Om_trans` composes each object on its parent's matrix after `Joint_trans2` has posed every character, and the draw takes that matrix (docs/internals/deviations/re2.md, `super_set`). The press's `cut_chg(133)` on 423 moves the cut to 5 with no load, so the original keeps the desk's close-up, darkening, until cut 5 loads on 459 (`main_15`), and arklay does the same ("The frames of a cut"). The frame means are 0.94 (`scene_115`), 0.90 (`scene_115_exit`) and 0.92 (`scene_115_line`, with its reference's frame 520 a repeat of 519, a late capture, at 38.7); before, they were about 2, with the close-up's 36 frames at 42 to 56. What is left is the close camera: Leon and Claire fill much of cuts 2, 5 and 6, and their models differ from the original's by about 1.2 a frame at their edges and in their lighting, where the room around them is 0.3. The runs' `frame_max_mean` is set a little above each mean.

## Locked doors

The check works as the original's does (#541). The pad has one button for the action, the shot and the confirm (raw `0x80`), which `Pad_set` gives all three `Key` bits, so `action` and `fire` in an input are the same button. A `pl_br_*` (`pl_br_00` to `pl_br_04`, and `pl_br_09` past its fourth step) raises `fg_status`'s `0x200000` when the button goes down (`Key_trg`) and neither the stairs nor a climb takes it. `Key_trg` is `Pad_set`'s: under `fg_stop & 0x1000000` (a door, the get-item close, a check's message) it keeps only `Key & 0x3C00`, and the first frame after the stop sets the last key to the current one, so a button held through a stop starts no check. `Psx_main` runs `Pad_set` on every frame, the status screen's, the map's and the box's included, so a button held through the status screen's close is no new press either. After `Player_move`, `Sce_at` checks the action areas from where the move left the player, at the point 620 units ahead, and the first that answers runs (`Sce_at_check(pPl, 1, 0x10)`); then every other area runs (`Sce_at_check(pPl, 1, 0)`). `Psx_main` clears the bit at the frame's end. So a player who aims, is hurt or is held checks nothing, and a shot that starts the aim from a stand can open a check. The same button is the confirm of the messages, the get-item window, the menus and room 200's computer: held, it speeds a message's text and answers the computer (`Key & 0x1000`), and pressed, it answers a message and confirms in the status screen, the item box and the map (`Key_trg & 0x1000`, `St_menu0_select` and `St_menu0_item`).

`Sce_Door` (`0x4E9460`) runs for a door's area when the action key's probe, 620 units in front of the player, lands in it. A locked door (bit `0x80` of its lock byte, its bit in `fg_key`, `0x98ED2C`, still clear) shows a door message (`Mess_set` with attr `0x100`, at `0xB90022`, stop `0xFF000000`) and plays a room-bank sound: 0x16 before "It's locked from inside." (key 0xFF) or before the message naming the lock of a key the player lacks (`key - 0x4C`), 0x26 after "You unlocked it." (key 0xFE), and 0x25 after "You have used the ..." when `Search_item_id` finds the key. An unlock and a key turn raise the door's bit, and the door opens on the next press. A key turn also sets `pG + 0x82B0`, the hook `Game_loop` calls before the scripts, to `Key_lost` (`0x4E95F0`) on the key's slot: on the first tick the message is down it takes one use off the key, and at none left it asks "This key is useless now. Discard?" (door message 9) and raises `fg_stop` itself, so nothing moves on that tick. Yes empties the slot and runs `Sort_item`, and No puts one use back. The message state (`fg_message`, `0x98504C`) is `0x80` while a message is up, with `0x40` once `Mess_disp` has typed a glyph (`Mess_flg`, which only `Mess_set` clears) and bit 0 for No; the trace's `msg` has it, and `fgkey` the two words of `fg_key`.

The runs warp in front of each door with `--no-enemies --life 200`, press the action key at 364, and compare `room,cam,rseed,p,mot,inv,snd,msg,fgkey` at `--offset 344`, with the input moved 344 ticks earlier and a setup script that puts the player where the original has them on tick 344. Room 204's spade door (the spec's first choice) starts the room's scene, which puts the player at `4181, 0, -17026`, while flag 4:85 is clear (event 6), and three recordings with `--flag 4:85` exited before play began; the runs use room 20B's heart door (lock 0x84, key 0x5B), which has no scene. All six match on every tick:

- `key_door_20B` (`--warp 20B:-1814:0:-12103:3029 --give 2:0x5B:1 --give 3:0x14:30`, `key_door_20B.txt`): the press plays 0x25 at 364 and the bit goes up (`fgkey=0800000000000000`); `msg` is `c0` on that tick, as `Mess_disp` types the first glyph after the moves, and the game holds from 365. The press at 600 closes the message (`msg=40`). On 601 `Key_lost` takes the key's last use (`5b:0`) and asks the question, and the player holds that tick. Yes at 800 plays 4:6, and on 801 the key's slot is gone and the rounds move up into it. The press at 900 opens the door, room 2:15 at 935 (`rseed` differs on the door's tick, as on every door). The frames from 360 to 999 have mean error 0.30, 0.22 with the message box blanked; the messages land on the original's pixels.
- `key_door_20B_keep` (the same, `key_door_20B_keep.txt`): right at 780 moves to No (4:4, `msg=c1`), the press at 800 answers it (4:5), and on 801 the key is back with one use.
- `key_door_207` (`--warp 207:-25770:0:-24897:1846 --give 2:0x5A:2`): the diamond key has two uses, so the close at 600 leaves `5a:1` on 601 with no question, and the press at 700 opens the door to room 209 at 735.
- `locked_door_20B` (`--warp 20B:-1814:0:-12103:3029`): 0x16 at 364 and the heart lock's message on two pages, turned at 500 and closed at 650; no bit goes up.
- `unlock_door_20A` (`--warp 20A:-15554:0:-15268:1866`): 0x26 at 364, on the message's tick, and bit 17 (`fgkey=0000400000000000`). The run doesn't go through: the door leads into room 200.
- `locked_inside_116` (`--warp 116:-26159:-7200:-25126:2048`, floor 4): 0x16 at 364 and "It's locked from inside."; no bit goes up.

## The typewriter

`Sce_Save` (`0x4E98F0`) runs for the typewriter's area (kind 9) when the action key's front point lands in it. It sets back routine slot 2 (`0x98E794`, which `Sce_Itembox` also uses) to `Sce_Save_backfunc` (`0x4E9930`) with state 0 (`0x98E542`), saves `fg_stop` (`0x991FC4`) and holds the world (`fg_stop | 0xFF000000`), and copies the area's first data byte, the save place, to `0x98E9A7`. `Game_loop` calls slot 2 right after `Sce_at`, so the routine's state 0 runs on the press tick: with an ink ribbon (`Search_item_id(0x1E)`, the id only) it shows door message 1 ("Will you use the Ink Ribbon?") and goes to state 1; without one it puts `fg_stop` back and shows door message 0. State 1 waits for the message to close, then clears the slot; No puts `fg_stop` back, and Yes sets game mode 1 and `fg_system | 0x40000`, which hand the room to the save screen. The trace's `tw` field has slot 2, the state byte and the place, and `gm` the game mode (`gi`'s first value).

The runs warp in front of the typewriter, left of the ink ribbons' area, with `--no-enemies --warp 200:-12200:-1800:-1900:1024 --flag 4:6 --life 200`, press the action key at 364, and compare `room,cam,rseed,p,mot,inv,ib,snd,msg,tw,gm` at `--offset 344`, with the input moved 344 ticks earlier and a setup script that puts the player where the original has them on tick 344 (`oracle/re2-regress.d/typewriter_200.toml`). Both match on every tick:

- `typewriter_200_no_ribbon` (`typewriter_200_no_ribbon.txt`): at 364 `tw=0,0,1` (the place is 1, "Hall"; it was the new game's 15 before) and `msg=c0`. The presses at 420 and 520 page through the message and close it (`msg=40`), and the walk from 560 moves the player.
- `typewriter_200_no` (`typewriter_200_no.txt`, `--give 2:0x1E:3`): at 364 `tw=4e9930,1,1`. After the pages, right at 510 moves to No (4:4, `msg=c1`) and the press at 540 answers it (4:5, `msg=41`); on 541 the slot is clear (`tw=0,0,1`) and the player is free again. The press at 600 asks again, with the cursor back on Yes, and it is answered No the same way at 780.

A Yes goes through `Game_loop`'s game modes, as START and the item box do, with step 5 starting `Mem_card` instead of `Status` (`fg_system & 0x40000`). Call the tick the back routine sees the Yes G (the answer's next tick). On G the game mode is 1, and `fg_stop` stays held. From G+1 it is 2: case 1 starts the fade out (`0x3000` a tick), and G+1 to G+3 are still `Game_loop` frames under `fg_stop` (the enemies' `Root_ck`, `pTbefore_func` and `Esp_move`'s stopped kinds), drawn at the status screen's open alphas (the room, 96, 192). Case 2 sees the fade's end on G+4 and holds black with the task asleep. On G+6 step 5 zeroes `fg_stop` and `0x6897C8`, keeps the word `Sce_Save` saved before its prompt (`0x991FC4`) in `0x6897C8`, clears `0x40000` and sets `0x4000` in `fg_system`, and starts `Mem_card`, which runs in the same pass. So the save the screen writes has `fg_stop` 0, and `Game_loop`'s step 1 zeroes it on a load anyway. START is refused from G (still under `fg_stop`'s `0x1000000` when it is tested) to the return. By the decompile, the screen's end X (`Cardaccess_exit`'s state 2, which sets `BYTE_0098f07b = 2`) wakes `Game_loop` on X+1 = R, whose step 9 runs game modes 3 and 5: the player restarts, `0x6897C8 |= fg_stop`, `fg_stop |= 0xEF000000` and the room fades in (`-0x1800` a tick, the status screen's return); on R+1 case 5 puts `0x6897C8` back in `fg_stop` and sets game mode 0.

- `typewriter_200_yes` (`typewriter_200_yes.txt`, `--give 2:0x1E:3`, 548 ticks, `--sound` and every frame) is `typewriter_200_no`'s first prompt without the move to No: the press at 540 answers Yes (`msg=40`, the decide cue 4:6:95:0), and on 541 the slot is clear and `gm=1` (`fgsys=40060800`). 542 to 547 have `gm=2` with `fgstop=ff000000` and the player, `rseed` and the inventory held, and on 547 `fgstop=00000000` and `fgsys=40024800`, the tick `Mem_card` starts. The run ends there, before the save screen draws anything (`Cardaccess_init`'s backdrop is on 547's frame, so the frames compared end on 546). It matches on every tick, with a frame mean of 0.07 over 380 to 546.
- `typewriter_200_yes_claire` is the same on Claire A (`--start claire`, `player = 1`, `character = 1`, and a setup with her start).

- `typewriter_200_yes_cancel` (`typewriter_200_yes_cancel.txt`, 760 ticks) takes the same Yes and cancels on the save screen at 600: `Mem_card` ends on 608 (X), and the return's game mode 5 is on 609 (R = X+1) with `fgstop=ef000000`, then game mode 0 with `fgstop` back to 0 on 610; the walk from 680 moves the player. It matches on every tick, with a frame mean of 0.19 ("The save screen").

## The save screen

The typewriter's Yes starts `Mem_card` (`0x4C57E0`) as task 1 on G+6; it suspends the game task and runs `Card_access` (`0x4C58A0`) a tick at a time in save mode (mode 0). The trace's `sv` field has its state (see "The trace"); `-` while task 1 runs anything else. On the screen's first tick `Cardaccess_init`'s step 0 loads the background and starts fade 0's fade in from black; step 1 waits for it (done 5 ticks later), its `Task_sleep(2)` skips a tick, and then each state runs on a tick of its own, the state shown being the one the tick left: 0 (the font, the folder), 1 (the folder's saves counted, the rows set), and 2, the list, from the tick after. A press in state 2 is taken on its own tick (the trace line shows the next state and the cue), and the next state runs on the tick after. On the way out `Cardaccess_exit` fades fade 0 out to black over 6 ticks (`sv`'s second value 1, then 2 on the seventh), and the tick after that `Mem_card` ends (X): `fgstat` shows `Swap_Cbuff`'s load (`0x100000`), and `Game_loop` runs the return on X+1 ("The typewriter").

The recorder empties the game's save folder (`savedata/`) before each record, or fills it from `--saves DIR`; `--keep-saves DIR` copies what the record left there. In the runner's manifest, `saves = true` keeps a run's saves in the reference's `saves/` folder (`meta.txt` lists each with its SHA-256), and `saves_from = "<run>"` starts a record with another reference's `saves/`. Classic Rebirth names a save `<player> <scenario>_<count>_<date> <hour>^<minute>.<second>.BIOHAZARD2` from the wall clock (`Leon A_01_2026-11-10 19^59.02.BIOHAZARD2`), 2048 bytes, and its label is the name with `:` for `^`. The label's length and the places of its spaces and `_` don't depend on the time, so neither do the reveal's ticks; the time's digits do differ between two recordings' frames.

The runs (`oracle/re2-regress.d/save_200.toml` and `typewriter_200_yes_cancel`) take `typewriter_200_yes`'s prefix (G = 541, `Mem_card` on 547) with an empty folder, compare `room,cam,rseed,rng,p,mot,inv,ib,snd,msg,tw,gm,fgstat,sv` and have every frame. In all three, `sv` is `3,1,0,...` (init's step 1) from 547, `3,2` on 552, state 1 on 554 and state 2 with 2 rows (new file and leave) from 555:

- `save_200_new` (`save_200_new.txt`, 1100 ticks): decide at 600 on the "new file" row (the top one, "Create New") plays `4:6:95:0` and its line shows state 3. On 601 the save is written: the ink ribbons go from 3 to 2 in `inv` and the save count to 1, and the state is 0x22. From 602 the label types a character on every tick the timer is 0 (`sv`'s eighth value), with `2:22:110:0` and the timer set to 4, or `2:24:70:0` and 8 after a space or `_`: 5 ticks a character, 9 after a space, and 29 characters by 758. On 763 the timer at 0 ends the reveal: state 0x24 with the timer at 60, which runs out on 823; 0x61 on 824, the exit's fade from 825, X on 832 and the return's game mode 5 on 833. The walk from 1000 moves the player. `saves/` holds the one save.
- `save_200_move` (`save_200_move.txt`, 1300 ticks): down held from 600 and up from 640 move the cursor every 4 ticks with `4:4`, wrapping between the two rows; cancel at 680 leaves with `4:5`. A second Yes at 940 opens the screen again, and decide on the leave row ("Exit") plays `4:5` and leaves as a cancel does.

What the frames show, in save mode: the title "Save" at the top, the list's rows centred under it with the cursor's grey bar on the chosen row, the darkened message window over the typewriter's background (`type00`), and, on a save row, the footer of its player (blue), count and place; the new file's row is "Create New" and has no footer. While the label types, the new row shows it in blue and the footer is the new save's (`Leon A/01/Hall`).

arklay's screen is `games/re2/ui/save_load.lua` (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "The save screen"), and the three runs match on every tick, the write tick's ribbon and count, every cue and the return included. Their frames match with a mean of 1.08 (`save_200_new`, whose worst frames are the label's time, the wall clock's on both sides), 0.40 (`save_200_move`) and 0.19 (`typewriter_200_yes_cancel`). arklay gives each run a save folder of its own, emptied first (`--save-dir`), so the screen lists no save, as the original's records start.

`oracle-re2.py patches` lists what Classic Rebirth changes in the save functions. `Mem_card`, `Cardaccess_exit`, `Save_push`, `FUN_00431D10` (the decide's row) and `Card_access`'s states 0, 1, 2, 3, 0x22, 0x24, 0x61 and 99 are the executable's. Patched: in `Card_access`, the overwrite choice's two wraps (`0x4C5EFD` 2 to 1, `0x4C5F1E` 3 to 2: a two-way choice), its decide's dispatch (`0x4C5F6B`, ten bytes of `NOP`) and a jump out of state 0x20's reveal (`0x4C6514`); in `Cardaccess_init`, the calls that load and convert `type00.adt` (`0x4C6CAC`, `0x4C6CC4`); in `Card_Mess_disp`, the title's y (`0x4C6E64`, 10 to 0x11), the overwrite choice's places (`0x4C6EC4`, `0x4C6EEC`), the `aOverwrite` print (`0x4C6FF3`, `NOP`), every string it prints (the executable's Japanese table repointed at Classic Rebirth's English, eleven pointers), the save row's footer and the reveal's (`0x4C717D`, `0x4C74D7`, calls into Classic Rebirth), the cursor bar's x and width (`0x4C74B5` 0x10 to 0x0A, `0x4C74C1` 0x120 to 0x12C) and a stack fix-up (`0x4C764A`); `SavePrint`, `FormatSaveName0` and `FormatSaveName1` jump to Classic Rebirth's own; `FUN_004315D0` and `PrintSaveList` lose a call each and print Classic Rebirth's strings; and `Get_mess_width` (`0x4C7CEA`) measures its text differently. So a save's states and ticks are the decompile's, and its drawing, its names and the overwrite choice are Classic Rebirth's, which only the recordings show.

## Weapons

Leon A's route (100, 101, 102, 118, 119, 11A, 11B, 103, 200) has three weapons: the handgun (0x02) and the knife (0x01) from the start, and the shotgun (0x07), the only weapon to pick up. The rooms' scripts (`arklay scd export`) have these weapon and ammunition pick-ups, with the run that takes each:

| Room | Area | Item | Flag (bank 8) | Taken in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 101 | 11 | the shotgun, 4 shells | 15 | `weapon_pickup_101` |
| 101 | 8 | handgun rounds (0x14), 15 | 0 | `weapon_pickup_101` |
| 101 | 9 | handgun rounds, 15 | 1 | `weapon_pickup_101` |
| 102 | 7 | handgun rounds, 30 (15 when system flag 25 is set) | 154 | `item_take` and the other item runs, `route_100_200_fight` |
| 118 | 4 | handgun rounds, 15, only when system flag 25 is set | 161 | none: the flag is clear in the route's game (`ck 0:25`, as in "Items") |
| 11A | 2 | handgun rounds, 15 | 195 | `route_100_200_fight` |
| 200 | 19 | handgun rounds, 30 (the shotgun with 5 in scenario B) | 152 | `weapon_pickup_200` |

Room 101's shotgun is on Kendo's body. `main_00` sets its area with five shells and puts it out at once (`aot_reset` to kind 0). Kendo's body only becomes checkable after the zombies' attack, which starts when the player walks into area 3, which arms area 2 by the counter. The first check of the body shows a message, and the second runs `aot_reset` on the area with four shells and then `aot_on`: the shotgun is picked up crouching, as the area's action byte is still 1, with four shells. No room on the route has shotgun shells. Nothing else on the route arms the player, and none of rooms 100, 119, 11B and 103 has a weapon or rounds.

The weapon runs (`weapon_*.txt`) use the inventory runs' room 102: record with `record --sound --no-enemies --warp 102:-11378:0:-4587:0 --life 200` and the run's `--give`, and run arklay with `--room 102 --player 0 --character 0 --sound`, the run's setup script and its `_arklay` input (the same input 344 ticks earlier), for the recording's ticks less 344. Compare at `--offset 344` with `--fields room,cam,rseed,p.x,p.y,p.z,p.angle,p.beh,p.as,p.hp,mot,inv,snd`. Arklay's `--room 102` start plays room 102's own sounds (type 3: 3:11, 3:0, 3:10 and 3:1, every 65 ticks), which the original's warp doesn't start, so drop each `3:` entry from `snd` in both traces first. The status screen's sounds are type 4, the core bank. At the item runs' spot the player is boxed in: a walk there runs on the spot, in both games, whichever way they face.

- `weapon_handgun.txt` with `--give 2:0x14:30 --ticks 1800` (`weapon_handgun.lua`): raise the handgun, fire the magazine's 18 rounds at the three tilts (shot 1:0, case 1:2), turn while aiming, let go of a shot early, lower and raise again, fire on empty, which reloads 18 from the 30 (1:4, then 1:3 and 1:e at 1403, 12 left), fire 8 more and lower. All 1456 ticks match, `snd` included.
- `weapon_empty.txt` with `--give 0:2:1 --ticks 700` (`weapon_empty.lua`): the last round, then the click (1:1) with nothing to reload. All 356 ticks match.
- `weapon_knife_wall.txt` with `--ticks 1300` (`weapon_knife_wall.lua`): START, right to the knife in slot 1, EQUIP, START; then slash at each tilt, with a wall in reach of every slash, so each one bounces at frame 7 (routine 6, clip 0x10, sound 1:0); the up and down slashes play their swish (1:5) first. Then held slashes, one after another.
- `weapon_knife.txt` with `--warp 102:-20000:0:15000:0 --ticks 1300` (`weapon_knife.lua`, which clears room 102's zombies, which stand at that spot, with their dead flags 51, 52, 54 and 55 in bank 6): the same EQUIP, a walk, then full slashes at the level and up tilts (1:5), a down slash that bounces (1:5, then 1:0), a slash let go of 12 ticks in, which ends at frame 20 and lowers, and a run of held slashes.
- `weapon_shotgun.txt` with `--give 2:7:5 --give 3:0x15:10 --ticks 1500` (`weapon_shotgun.lua`): START, down to the shotgun in slot 2, EQUIP, START; raise, fire the five shells at the three tilts (shot 1:0, pump 1:4, shell 1:9, in that order when the last two share a tick), fire on empty, which reloads 5 from the 10 (1:3 twice), fire twice more, letting go of aim during the second shot (the gun lowers when the shot ends), then raise, fire once and lower.

- `weapon_pickup_101.txt` (`record --no-enemies --keep-enemy-in 101:0 --flag 4:135 --ticks 7100`, no `--sound`; arklay from the new game with `route_100_200.lua`, as `route_100_200`) is `route_100_200.txt` to tick 2980, then into room 101's areas 3 and 2, which start the zombies' attack on Kendo (the scene runs with the zombies switched off). It then checks his body twice: the first check shows a message, which holds `fg_stop` at `0xFFFF0000`, so the player, the enemies and the scripts stop until it is dismissed. The second check makes the player crouch and take the shotgun with four shells. The run then takes the handgun rounds on both shelves (15 and 15 into one stack of 30), EQUIPs the shotgun, fires its four shells at the three tilts and fires twice on empty, with no shells to load. Without `--sound`, since the attack's voice lines end by DirectSound's clock in a `--sound` record. All 7100 ticks match on `room`, `cam`, `rng`, `rseed`, the player, `mot`, `inv`, Kendo (`em`) and `pneck`, apart from room 100's door tick and the head's 1 to 3 units after each window's and the status screen's close (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "The head after the get-item window"). This needed four fixes: the check's message holding `fg_stop`, `aot_reset` keeping an area's last two data bytes so that the shotgun is still taken crouching, the crouch from a script's `aot_on` starting in the scripts' pass, and the window's close holding the next tick's keys, as a door's does. While the status screen is up, the original's `Cut_no` is also 0 from the open's sixth tick to the close's eighth, as in the get-item window.
- `weapon_pickup_200.txt` with `--warp 200:-8950:-1800:-700:3072 --flag 4:6 --give 0:2:1 --ticks 1300` (`weapon_pickup_200.lua`; flag 4:6 skips the hall's entry scene): take the 30 handgun rounds on the counter, fire the handgun's last round, fire on empty, which reloads 18 from them (12 left), fire twice more and lower. All 956 ticks match, `snd` and `scene` included. arklay's setup script re-runs the room's start for the flag: the run's first `--room` start, with 4:6 still clear, begins the entry scene, which raises `fg_status`'s scene bit and puts up `fg_stop`'s input bit. The re-run clears `fg_stop`, and the second start clears `fg_status` as `Set_room` does (`& 0xFFF04000`), so the scene's letterbox bars don't come in. The frames from tick 350 are compared: the original shows cut 0 unscrolled, as arklay does.

- `colt_118.txt` with `--warp 118:-24617:0:-24996:1024 --life 200 --equip-load 0xD --give 0:0xD:6 --give 1:0x14:60 --ticks 1800` (`colt_118.lua`, run_119's start; `oracle/re2-regress.d/colt.toml`): aim the Colt S.A.A. at room 118's zombies and fire every 40 ticks. The raise turns to the nearest at 0x120 a tick, the shot plays 1:0, and an empty Colt reloads six on the next press, throwing its cases out (`Esp_56`). All 1456 ticks match, `inv`, `snd` and the six zombies included, and so do the effect pool (`esp_colt_118`) and the frames (mean 0.04).
- `licker_204_fight.txt` with the Colt, `--warp 204:-15400:0:-8000:3072 --flag 4:85 --life 2000 --equip-load 0xD --give 0:0xD:6 --give 1:0x14:200 --ticks 2400` (`colt_licker_204.lua`): the handgun fight's input, reloading every six shots. All 2056 ticks match, `inv`, `snd`, `pneck`, the zombie and the licker included, and the frames (mean 0.41). The effect pool (`esp_colt_licker_204`) matches on every tick too: the slash's blood starts at part 13's `Workm.t` as the last draw left it, 200 lower and not turned by the part (`switchD_004651fd::caseD_9`), and the jump's bite's blood is made on the player's part 0 as the last draw left it, after the pushes, and stays on it (`FUN_00466100`, `FUN_00468710`). The handgun's fight on the effect pool (`esp_licker_204_fight`) matches on every tick as well, the shots' kind 2 effects included (#700).
- `smg_equip_118.txt` with `--warp 118:-24617:0:-24996:1024 --life 200 --give 2:0xF:100 --give 3:0x1B:100 --ticks 1800` (`smg_equip_118.lua`, run_119's start; `oracle/re2-regress.d/burst.toml`): EQUIP the sub-machine gun from the status screen, aim at 620 and fire 60-tick bursts every 80 ticks from 640. `pl_mv05_w2` holds the burst (clips 0x10, 0xA and 0xD), `At_tbl_top`'s sequence looks every third tick, `Tama_decrease_i` takes two rounds every eighth call, and `pl_w0f_t2_move` makes the flash, the smoke and a case every sixth tick. All 1456 ticks match, `inv`, `snd` and the six zombies included, and the frames too (mean 0.14). So does the effect pool (`esp_smg_equip_118`), now that a thrown part falls and lands as `Parts_down` has it (#491 slice 6).
- `flame_equip_118.txt`, the same with `--give 2:0x10:100 --give 3:0x17:100` (`flame_equip_118.lua`): the flamethrower throws a flame (`Esp_46`) every third tick, which burns the zombies it reaches. Its start and go-on sounds play at part 11's `Pos`, as the original passes it to `Snd_se_on`. All 1456 ticks match, the effect pool included, except that the reload's frame sound (1:e) pans one less than the original's on tick 1384, which the manifest ignores.
- `spark_equip_118.txt` as Claire, with `--start claire --warp 118:-24617:0:-24996:1024 --life 200 --give 2:0xE:100 --ticks 1800` (`spark_equip_118.lua`, `bowgun_118`'s start; `oracle/re2-regress.d/spark.toml`): EQUIP the spark shot from the status screen, aim at 620 and fire every 60 ticks from 640. Each shot looks for a body before it fires (`pl_mv05_w3_t2`), finds a zombie, takes a round, then on its fourth frame holds on it (clip 0xB) and takes two more, while `Esp_0E` strikes it, strings its sparks to it and flashes the screen. All 1456 ticks match, `inv`, `snd` and the six zombies included, and so do the effect pool (`esp_spark_equip_118`) and the frames (mean 0.10, the flashes 0.04).
- `clairegun_118.txt` with `--start claire --warp 118:-24617:0:-24996:1024 --life 200 --give 2:0x3:15 --ticks 700` (`clairegun_118.lua`, `bowgun_118`'s start with the handgun in slots 0 (13 rounds, equipped) and 2 (15 rounds); `oracle/re2-regress.d/clairegun_118.toml`): EQUIP the second handgun, aim at 620 and fire from 640. `St_com_use_equip` (`0x4F8620`) compares the cursor's slot with the equipped slot, not item ids, so the second copy is equipped (a copy of the weapon in hand isn't taken off), and the shot spends that slot's rounds (`inv` 3:13,1:1,3:14). arklay keeps the equipped slot in `equipped_slot`. It matches on every field for all 356 ticks. Moving stacks (combine, box, swap) doesn't carry the slot; `set_equipped` falls back to the first stack holding the item.
- `rocket_equip_118.txt` with `--warp 118:-24617:0:-24996:1024 --life 200 --give 2:0x11:4 --ticks 1400` (`rocket_equip_118.lua`, run_119's start; `oracle/re2-regress.d/rocket.toml`): EQUIP the rocket launcher from the status screen, aim at 620 and fire every 100 ticks from 640. Four rockets fly (`Esp_18`), from the presses at 640, 740, 840 and 1040; the press at 940 comes during a shot and the one at 1140 finds it empty and clicks. The first and the last kill a zombie each, the first's head flying off behind it (`FUN_00458d40`). From the first hold `fg_status`'s `0x40000` stays up. All 1056 ticks match, `inv`, `snd` and the six zombies included, and so do the effect pool (`esp_rocket_equip_118`) and the frames (mean 0.18).
- `gatling_equip_118.txt` with `--warp 118:-24617:0:-24996:1024 --life 200 --give 2:0x12:100 --ticks 1400` (`gatling_equip_118.lua`, run_119's start; `oracle/re2-regress.d/gatling.toml`): EQUIP the gatling gun from the status screen, aim at 620 and fire from 640 in holds of 70 to 120 ticks, turning left and right while it fires, letting go of fire alone (the spin down, from which a held fire spins up again) and of aim with it. `pl_mv05_w5_t2` spins up (clip 0xA, 1:b), fires (clip 0xB) with `Weapon_at_ck` on two ticks in three, and spins down (clip 0xD, 1:c); it never takes a round, as its endless flag is taken as up (the gatling bonus), so `fg_status`'s `0x800000` comes up with the first spin. All 1056 ticks match, `inv`, `snd` and the zombies included, and so do the effect pool (`esp_gatling_equip_118`) and the frames (mean 0.15).

These five EQUIP the weapon from the status screen because `--equip-load` sets the weapon before the warp but doesn't load its PLW: the original then plays the handgun's clips (PLW02). Those match the Colt's (PLW0D) but not these five weapons' clips.

In the three EQUIP runs every field matches on every tick, `rseed` and `snd` included: the status screen opens and closes as in the inventory runs (START at 364, the close at 480, the player's move restarted at 490 and the world back at 491), and `weapon_handgun` and `weapon_empty` still match on every tick.

## Films

The recorder writes `MovieEnable = 0` to `config.ini`, which the executable reads inverted (`MarniConfig::ReadAll` keeps `GetDword("MovieEnable", 1) == 0`), so the original runs with its films off: a script's `movie_on` (`Init_movie_work`, 0x4CAEC0) raises `fg_system`'s `0x200`, closes the sound (`SsClose`), opens it again (`SsInit2`) and drops `0x200` inside the one call, and `Movie_set(2)` leaves two frames unpresented. No film plays, and nothing in the trace but the `movie` field shows it. arklay's traces follow the same config; its interactive window plays the film. With `--sound` the original's `SsInit2` is slow, but the tick after `movie_on` still traces `Movie_idx` 0, so no tick of the hold depends on the pace.

- `film_204.txt` with `--warp 204:-15400:0:-8000:3072 --flag 4:85 --life 200 --no-enemies --keep-enemy 1 --ticks 1000` (`film_204.lua`; flag 4:85 skips the room's entry scene, which would move the player, and only the scene's licker, slot 1, is left on): walk up the corridor into area 7, whose event 9 is the licker window scene. The scene starts at tick 395 with `cut_chg 7`, changes to cut 4 at 424 and runs `movie_on 10` at 425 (`movie=0000,1,0` on both ticks, 0 at 426), then drops the licker in at 426. In the original the screen keeps the frame from before the first change for 424, 425 and 426, and arklay's does too; every traced field matches on all 656 ticks (`film_204`). The frames differ at 395 and 396, where the original holds the frame before `cut_chg 7` and arklay draws the old cut with the player already moved. In the `.wav` the room's ambience (`sub04`, loaded and silent before) becomes audible from `movie_on`'s tick: `SsInit2` starts the sub again, on a new buffer, at DirectSound's full level.

## The trace

| Field | Source |
|---|---|
| `room` | `current_stage + 1`, `current_room` |
| `rng` | `Rnd`'s state, the low 16 bits of `0x00988610` |
| `p` | `pl`: `m.t` for x, y and z, `cdir.y & 0xFFF`, `routine_0`, `routine_1`, `life` |
| `cam` | `current_cut` |
| `e` | each `enemies[n]` with bit 0 of `be_flg` set, as slot `n + 1`: id, position, angle, `routine_0`-`routine_2`, `life`, then three zeros |
| `pad` | the pad value given this tick |
| `fgsys`, `fgstop` | `fg_system` and `fg_stop`: the original's only. |
| `fgcom` | `fg_common` (`0x98EB4C`), bank 4, its 32 bytes in hex, memory's order: each word's low byte first, so index `i` is bit `0x80 >> (i & 7)` of byte `4 * (i >> 5) + 3 - ((i & 31) >> 3)` (3:128 is byte 19's `0x80`, which every `*_303` reference shows). |
| `fgstat` | `fg_status` (`0x989ED0`), bank 1, one word in hex. arklay writes its bank 1's word. Compared, when `--fields` names it, through `FGSTAT_COMPARED` (`0xFFEF7E3F`) on both sides, without 0x40, 0x80, 0x100, 0x8000 and 0x100000, the bits arklay keeps outside the bank (a message or a hit, `pl_mv_05`, the camera's lock, the get-item window and the status screen, `Swap_Cbuff`'s load). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `fgscn` | `fg_scenario` (`0x98EB2C`), bank 3, as `fgcom`. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `fgroom` | `fg_room` (`0x98EB6C`), bank 5's first word in hex (5:0 to 5:31; `fg_tick`, 5:32 up, isn't traced). `Sce_work_clr_set` clears it when the room loads, which can be many ticks after `room=` changes (91 in `route_100_200`'s doors, one in `door_101_102`'s). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `fgitem` | `fg_item` (`0x98EBB4`), bank 8, as `fgcom`: the pick-ups taken in rooms 1xx-4xx (`scd_var_stage` 0 to 3; `St_get_item`, `St_get_file`). In references recorded from this change on: `item_take`, `item_crouch_103`, `weapon_pickup_200`, `file_pickup_201`, `file_pickup_208`, `file_pickup_115` and `leon_a_200_out` name it, and a machine without those seven references recorded again gets the runner's refusal for them. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `fgitem2` | `fg_item_2` (`0x98EBD4`, 44 bytes), bank 34: the pick-ups of rooms 5xx-7xx. The original's only; arklay doesn't model the bank |
| `mot` | the player's `routine_2`, `routine_3`, `Move_no`, `Move_cnt`, `Hokan_flg`, `D_life_u` and speed x, y, z |
| `em` | enemy slot 0 when it's a character (id 0x40 up): id, x, y, z, `cdir.y & 0xFFF`, `routine_0`-`routine_3`, `Move_no`, `Move_cnt`, `Hokan_flg`, `D_life_u` and speed x, y, z; `scripts/oracle.py` compares all but the speed |
| `es` | `splayer_work`, Sherry and the other second players, with `em`'s fields |
| `zp` | for each enemy `zm` lists whose parts aren't all drawn and at `0x808080`: slot, the mask of parts `0..Parts_num` (`+0x107`) whose `Be_flg` bit 0 is clear, part 0's `Poly_rgb` (`0x00BBGGRR`) and the wrapping sum of every part's, from `pSin_parts_ptr` (`+0x198`, `0xAC` bytes a part); `-` for none. A licker's `Parts_num` is 23: its tongue, part 22, is one. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `spl` | `splayer_work`'s own words for `Spl_Auto`: `Type` (hex), the follow point (`+0x218`, `+0x21A`), its state (`+0x21C`, hex), distance (`+0x21E`) and wall turn (`+0x220`), the wall count (`+0x224`), the touch count (`+0x223`), the wait (`+0x22A`), `Timer0` to `Timer3` (`+0x158`, `+0x15A`, `+0x16A`, `+0x16B`), `Dest`, `Life`, `Max_life`, `Sca_info & 1`, `Root_ck`'s turn (`+0x150`), `Spl_pos_ck_timer` (`0x53CDEF`), `Neck_flg` (hex) and `At_em_flg` (hex); `-` with no character there. `Timer2` and `Timer3` hold what the work's memory held until a state writes them, and aren't compared. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `spe` | what the partner's `Spl_enemy_ck` left: the nearest-enemy cache's three distances (`0x692FC8`) and the slots of the three works it listed (`0x692FE0`, `-` for none), then after a `|` each enemy `zm` lists as its slot, `L_spl` (`+0x1F4`, hex: the distance, with its place on the list in the top two bits) and, when listed, `Dir_spl` (`+0x1F8`); `-` for no enemy. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `spc` | the partner's climb and water words: `Water` (`+0x10C`), `nFloor` (`+0x106`), `Damage_cnt` (`+0x1D3`) and `Be_flg`'s 4 (`+0`); `-` with no second player. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `neck` | the player's `Neck_flg`, `Neck_no`, whether it aims at another work, and the joint's turn, rates, limits and rotation; arklay writes the first five |
| `pneck` | where the player's head is in the world, the neck joint's `+0x5C`, which `Root_ck` aims at; `-` when arklay hasn't posed it. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `bgm` | `Bgm`, each of the three `Seq_ctr`'s state and hold, and the tracks of the five channels (`bgm/<track>`), `-` for none; it says something only with `--sound` |
| `xavol` | `xa_vol`'s level for the voice channel (`0x693E88`), which the next line's start gives DirectSound. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `movie` | `fg_system & 0x8200` (a film plays, and the bit `Init_movie_work` clears), `Movie_idx` (`0x6805C0`, the frames `WinMain` still holds after this tick's decrement) and `Movie_r0` (`0x68058E`, the film's state) (see "Films"). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `inv` | `Item_work`'s ten slots and the personal item, `id:count`, then the slot count (see "Items"). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `pl` | the player character and its stash (see "The player character" below): `pPl.Id` (`0x989EF8`), `scd_var_next_pld` (`0x98EB24`), `Max_life` (`pPl+0x162`), the equipped slot and weapon (`St+8` and `St+0xA`, `0x691F68` and `0x691F6A`; slot 128 for none), the stash's slot and size (`0x9888DA`, `0x9888DB`), the stashed life (`0x98E9B6`), `poison_status` (`0x98A108`) and `poison_timer` (`0x98A10A`), all decimal. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `gi` | the game mode, the status screen's routines, the get-item window's routine and sub-step and the message state; the original's only |
| `fgcom` | `fg_common` (`0x98EB4C`), the common flag bank: its eight words as their bytes in hex. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `pc` | room 200's computer: task 1's `R0`, `R1`, `R2` and sleep count, `c_mode` (`0x98E52D`), the frame's direction and step, `printed`, `confirmed` and the console's column and row; `-` unless task 1 runs `Windows` (`0x4D1F50`). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `fl` | the FILE page: `St_menu0_file`'s work bytes, the binder and slot (`0x691F71`, `0x691F72`), the page (`0x691F73`), the shared blink's level and way (`0x691F88`, `0x691F89`; the item cursor's pulse and MAP's marker too), the zoom (`0x691F8E`), the spin (`0x691F90`) and the page's x (`0x691FBC`), then `/` and `File_tag_bk` (`0x98EE60`, the files held, three binders of eight, `ff` for none) as hex. `--file INDEX:FILE` gives the original's run a file. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `ib` | the item box: `Item_box_entrance`'s state (`0x98E542`), the lid's speed and step (`0x689C90`, `0x689C98`) and its turn (the box's object's `Cdir_z`), then the screen's routine, sub, count, cursor and START gate (`0x691F61` to `0x691F63`, `0x691F6C`, `0x691F76`), the box's top row and scroll (`0x691F84`, `0x691F85`) and the chosen row's pulse (`0x692FBE`). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `box` | the inventory's two-slot marks (`Item_work`'s third bytes), then the item box's filled slots, `slot:id:count:mark`, joined with `;`, or `-`. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `msg` | the message state, `fg_message` (`0x98504C`), hex: `0x80` while a message is up, `0x40` once it has typed a glyph, bit 0 a question's No (see "Locked doors"). The original's is `gi`'s sixth field, which `oracle.py` reads when there is no `msg`. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `fgkey` | the doors' lock bits, `fg_key`'s two words (`0x98ED2C`, `0x98ED30`), hex. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `tw` | back routine slot 2 (`0x98E794`: 0, `4e9a20` for `Item_box_entrance`, `4e9930` for `Sce_Save_backfunc`), its state (`0x98E542`) and the save place (`0x98E9A7`) (see "The typewriter"). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `gm` | the game mode (`Game`, `0x991F80`), `gi`'s first value on the original's side. arklay's follows only the typewriter's path, so only typewriter runs name it. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `sv` | the save screen while task 1 runs `Mem_card` (`0x4C57E0`), else `-`: `Card_access`'s step (task 1's `+9`), `Cardaccess_init`'s or `Cardaccess_exit`'s step (`+10`), the state (`0x689A9C`'s high byte, hex), the mode (its low byte: 0 save, 1 load), `Cursor` (`0x9864B4`), the top row (`0x986390`), the rows shown (`0x986394`), the timer (`0x986388`), the overwrite's choice (`0x98638C`), the typed count (`0x689AA4`), the folder's saves (`cnt0`, `0x663188`) and the save count (`0x98E9BC`'s low word) (see "The save screen"). arklay writes the screen script's work (`games/re2/ui/save_load.lua`) and the save count. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `hk` | the per-tick hook `pG + 0x82B0` (`0x98E790`: 0 none, 1 `Hikidashi_entrance`, 2 `Key_lost`, 9 another), its state byte (`0x98E541`), then each room object's `Be_flg & 0x11` (`0x98A61C`, 0x1F8 a slot, below the RDT's object count), hex, joined with `/`. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `pon` | the slot of the room object the player stands on or climbs onto (`pOn_om`, `0x98A0D4`), or `-` (see "Climbing onto an object"). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `om` | each room object (`0x98A61C`, 0x1F8 a slot, below the RDT's object count): `n:Be_flg,x,y,z,Cdir_y,Push_cnt,At_sce_no,nFloor`, `Be_flg` in hex, joined with `;` (see "Pushing"). `scripts/oracle.py` compares it as `om<n>`; compared only when `--fields` names them, `om*` for all |
| `atsce` | the player's `At_sce_no` (`0x989EFB`, script member 9), hex: the last area `Sce_at_check` found the player in, `ff` after `Sce_work_clr_at`. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `fguse` | `fg_use` (`0x989ED8`), bank 10's first four words as their bytes: the key items the areas under the player want, which `Sce_work_clr` clears after every scheduler pass. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `scv` | the script variables 0 to 3 (`0x98EAE4`), hex: the forward area, the body area, the used item (`scd_var_used_item`) and variable 3, which `Sce_work_clr` sets back to `ffff`. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `scene` | `fg_status & 0x10`: a scene is up, the bit that brings in the letterbox bars. arklay writes it; `scripts/oracle.py` takes it from the original's `fgstat`. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `snd` | each sound started on the tick, `type:index:volume:pan`, joined with `/`; with `--sound` only. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `esp` | `Esp_` (`0x992CA0`, 0x60 slots of 0x7C bytes): the live count (`+0x18`'s 0x8000), then each slot whose `+0x18` isn't 0 (the slots `Esp_call`'s free search sees as taken, deferred ones included) as `slot:anim,update,kind,row,flags,frame,x,y,z`: `+0x00`, `+0x01`, `+0x1C`, `+0x1E`, `+0x18` and `+0x21` in hex, and `+0x34`, the place `Esp_pos` writes in the world, in decimal; joined with `;`, or `-`. A dead slot keeps its kind (`Esp_init_R` and `Esp_kill` clear only the flags), so the flags decide. `scripts/oracle.py` compares it as `espn` (the count), `espk` (the kinds and rows that are out, without their slots: one effect missing on one side moves the slot of every later one, as the search goes from the top) and `esp<slot>`; `esp` selects all three. Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `mapfg` | the MAP page's banks in hex: `fg_map`'s five words (bank 9, `0x98EC04`), then the first words of `fg_map_c` (bank 32), `fg_map_i` (bank 33) and the floors' bank 35 (`0x98EC00`). Compared only when `--fields` names it |
| `map` | the MAP page's shown floor (`0x691F7A`), the marker's blink and its direction (`0x691F88`, `0x691F89`) while `0x691F61` is 4, else `-`; the original's only |

`rseed`, `fade` and `f` are placeholders so the line parses: `rseed` and the fade
have no RE2 equivalent recorded yet, and the frame isn't hashed. A screenshot of
any tick is available through `--shot-dir` and `--shot-every`.

### The player character

A room script's `save(32, N)` makes the player another character on the next room's load: `Set_room` (`0x4DE7B0`, case 10) compares `scd_var_next_pld` with `pPl.Id` and, when they differ, swaps the inventory (`St_chenge_pl`), loads the character (`Player_set`) and stashes or restores the life and poison. Leon A's sewer hands the player to Ada (0xE) at 303's door into 30B and back in 305. The `pl` field shows it: the id and variable 32, which are equal outside a pending change (both 0 for Leon, 1 for Claire), `Max_life`, the equipped slot and weapon, the stash's slot and size, the stashed life (200 at a new game; `Save_push` writes it on every save) and the poison. arklay writes the same from `Re2State` (`src/game/pl_change2.rs`) on every tick of an RE2 room. No run names it yet; the runs of the Ada parts of Leon A's route will. A setup script starts as Ada with `api:re2_change_player(14)`, and reads or writes variable 32 with `api:re2_var(32)` and `api:set_re2_var(32, n)`.

## What compares today

`scenario` compares `room`, `cam`, the player's x, y, z, angle, `beh`, `as` and
health, and `mot` (`--fields`). The enemy and presentation fields are opt-in:
`rng` for the shared random stream, `e`, `em` and `es` for the enemy slots,
`zm<slot>` for one enemy slot's work and `zm*` for every slot's,
`om<n>` for one room object and `om*` for every one,
`neck` for the player's neck, the `snd` and `die` traces for sound and the
game over, and `esp` (or `espn` and `espk` alone) for the effect pool. arklay emits `fgcom`, `fgscn`, `fgitem`, `fgstat` and `fgroom` (the banks), `scene`, `fg_status`'s scene bit, and `mapfg`, the MAP page's banks; it doesn't write `fgsys` or `fgstop`. Add each new field to
the default in `RE2_FIELDS` when the milestone that makes it comparable lands.

## Classic Rebirth

The game runs with Classic Rebirth. The tool turns off Classic Rebirth's gameplay options in its own
copy of `config.ini` (quick turn, tactical reload, auto aim, cutscene skip, debug
mode). With those off, `patches` shows no changes to `Pl_move`, the weapon
routines or `Game_loop`. What remains in the player, door, room and enemy code is
calls redirected for loading files, drawing and sound.

## The title

`record --boot` traces from boot rather than from play: the input's ticks count from the first tick the tool sees, no title route is played, and a line is written for every tick, with `--shot-dir` frames named `t<tick>.png` as for play. Its lines hold the title's state (`Title`, `0x5035B0`): `ti=` is `title_mv_state`, `Ti_Disp_add`, task 0's init step and sub-step (`+8`, `+9` of `0x68A220`), the menu's page and step (`0x691B82`, `0x691B83`), `Ti_Sel_mode`, `Ti_Cur`, the cursor's pulse (`0x691B88`), the menu's slide count (`0x691B8A`) and `demo_countdown`; `tlogo=` is the white tiles' level, the logo's offset and its slide count (`0x691B96` to `0x691B9C`); `tfade=` is fade slot 0's level, step and blend (`0x98F07C`), then `Ti_kido` and `Ti_Add`; `tsel=` is what the new-game choice sets: `fg_system` masked to `0x20060` (EASY's `0x20`, `censorship_off`'s `0x40` and the difficulty confirm's `0x20000`, in hex), `hard_mode` (`0x6805B1`) and `censorship_off` (`0x6805B3`); and `snd=` and `bgm=` as in play. `tx=` is task 0's second sub-step (`+0xA`), the byte `0x691B84`, the slide's two counts (`0x691B8B`, `0x691B8C`, which the 4th Survivor's title uses for its name and its logo), EX.BATTLE's level page (`0x691EF2`, `0x691EF3`) and level (`0x989E91`, 2 from the boot), `fg_system` masked to `0x01020009` and `fg_status` masked to `0x02004000` (both hex); `tfade2=` and `tfade3=` are fade slots 2 and 3, as `tfade`'s first three. `tsel` is in the recordings from `newgame_arrange_hard` on, and `tx`, `tfade2` and `tfade3` in those from `special_4th` on; the earlier ones don't have them. `record --config KEY=VALUE` sets a key of the game's `config.ini` for the run, such as `SPECIAL=0x1`: the game reads its numbers in hex, so write the `0x`, or it reads something else (`SPECIAL=1` came back as 0x44, every extra). arklay's `--ui title --trace` writes the same fields from the values `games/re2/ui/title.lua` publishes, starting at the title's first tick.

The boot shows the warning screen (`Capcom_logo`'s steps 5 to 7, fading in and out on fade slot 0) and reaches `Title_init` on tick 189, which is arklay's tick 0. The warning sets `fg_system` bit 2, so `Title_init` takes the branch that starts white (slot 0 added at 0x7F80 with no step, the logo at 240), step 5 then runs once (tick 190: the logo's slide from 0 up to -46 over 22 ticks, and two bands of 320 by 40 at the top and bottom taken away at the white tiles' level, 0x36), and the menu runs from tick 191. The runner's `title_*` runs compare `ti,tlogo,tfade,snd` at `offset = 189` with the input moved 189 ticks earlier:

- `title_menu.txt` moves the cursor down through the five rows, once more at the last, and back up past the first, over 700 ticks. Every field matches on every tick from 189, the cursor's cue included (core slot 4 at volume 110: `Snd_se_on` gives every core sound 0x6E while `0x99CF6C` is clear, as `Title_init` leaves it). Frame for frame the mean is 0.06.
- `title_load.txt` confirms LOAD GAME on tick 260: the confirm's two cues (core slots 0 and 1), fade slot 0 added from 0x7800 down by 0x800 a tick (the white flash), then from 277 taken away from 0x200 up by 0x80 a tick, and on 530 the title hands over to the load screen (`Ti_Disp_add` 2, `Mem_card`). Every field matches through 530; frames 0.04 (one frame of the original's, 208, was captured between two frames, 0.92).
- `title_option.txt` confirms OPTION on tick 400: the decide cue (slot 6), a fade to black by 0x1800 a tick, and on 409 step 4 starts the option task (`0x44A040`), which leaves `title_mv_state` 1 for the title to start again when it ends. arklay has no option screen, so its title starts again at once (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "The title"). Every field matches through 408; frames 0.06.
- `title_special.txt` confirms SPECIAL on tick 380 and cancels on 500: the row slides up by 37 over 8 ticks while the others fade, the five extras show under it (`Special` is 15 in this install, the executable's own start value), and the cancel slides back to the rows with the cursor on SPECIAL. While it slides the original draws its choices through case 0 of `0x505FC0`, a lone EXIT at 120, 160, because the case is a register its caller left (ECX, which `Fade_status(0)` leaves 0); arklay does the same. Every field matches; frames 0.06.
- SPECIAL's extras (`oracle/re2-regress.d/special_extras.toml`). `special_4th.txt` confirms 4TH on 440: the decide cue, then slot 2 fades to black from 441 (`0x504900`'s steps 4 and 5, 0x200 up by 0x800), and on 457, the tick it is done, `fg_system` gains 8 and `fg_status` flag 1:6 (`FUN_004B73C0`) and `title_mv_state` goes to 3. On 458 `Load_title_survivor` runs in one tick: core bank 0x12, `spy_logo.tim` as texture 0x15, slots 0 to 2 off and slot 3 at 0x7C00 over `title_o.adt`, which it draws behind the sprites (`AddTile`'s back list). From 459 the name (word 21) fades in by 2 a tick until 522, the logo (words 17 to 20) by 1 until 650, then slot 3 lifts by 0x100 a tick and on 741 the decide cue plays and START and EXIT show. START on 1000 plays the confirm's two cues, the white flash on slot 0 to 1015 and the fade from 1016 by 0x80 to 1267; on 1269 `title_mv_state` is 2 and `Ti_Disp_add` 3, the new game's hand-off, which arklay doesn't port: it ends the run there (`ticks = 1270`). Every field matches; frames 0.03. `special_4th_exit.txt` skips the build-up with a confirm and takes EXIT (the cancel cue, `Init_global`, a 6-tick fade, core bank 0x10 again and `Title_init` from black), `special_tofu.txt` takes TO-FU (`fg_system` 9, core bank 0x13, `tofulogo.tim` and `title_o2.adt`, the name 16 right and 4 up) and its START, to its hand-off on 1310. Both match on every tick, frames 0.04 and 0.02 (the TO-FU run up to 1309). SPECIAL's other layouts (config.ini's `SPECIAL` below 15, arklay's `--title-special N`, a run's `special` key) have no run: with `SPECIAL` at 0x0, 0x1, 0x2 or 0x5 the original exits before its first traced tick, on Linux and on the VPS alike, so they are checked against `0x504900` and `0x505FC0` and by the unit tests only.

The words are drawn twice, once in blend 1 (added) and a line lower in blend 0 (averaged), and same-depth sprites are drawn last added first, as the original's ordering table does; the copyright line is opaque (`Moji_License` clears the sprite's code). The PC renderer expands the sheets' 5-bit colours as 8v + 7 and the background's as arklay does, and it draws fade blend 2 as a product (the frame times 1 - level / 255) rather than a subtraction, so `api:fade_tile` does too.

## The new-game choice

The original has no screen of its own for the choice: on the title, ORIGINAL MODE's or ARRANGE MODE's confirm plays the decide cue (core slot 6) and sets menu page 5 or 6, whose step 0 slides the rows away (`0x691B8A` up to 8 over 8 ticks) and shows LEON and CLAIRE with EXIT under them (`0x504240`, drawn by `0x505C00`); left and right pick the character, a confirm on it goes to the difficulty (page 1 or 2, with no slide: `0x5044D0`): NORMAL and EASY side by side, and under them EXIT and, with `SuperHardSwitch` on as in this install, HARD beside it (down goes from NORMAL to HARD and from EASY to EXIT). EXIT or the cancel key plays the cancel cue (slot 5) and slides back to the title's rows, the cursor on ORIGINAL MODE from the character and on the mode chosen from the difficulty. The difficulty's confirm plays the confirm's two cues, sets `fg_system` bit 17 and `Ti_Sel_mode` 1, and leaves through the title's white flash and slow fade; `Ti_Disp_add` 3 then starts the game. The runs compare `ti,tlogo,tfade,snd` as the `title_*` runs do (offset 189):

- `newgame_leon.txt`: ORIGINAL MODE on tick 300 (the slide 301 to 308), LEON on 360, NORMAL on 420 (the flash from 421, the fade 437 to 689), and the hand-off on 690. Every field matches through 690; frames 0.10 (the original's 429 and 432 repeat the frame before, 14 and 16).
- `newgame_claire.txt`: CLAIRE, then on the difficulty right to EASY, down to EXIT and back up, and EASY's confirm on 510; the hand-off on 780. Every field matches through 780; frames 0.04.
- `newgame_arrange_hard.txt`: ARRANGE MODE on tick 330, which sets `censorship_off` and `fg_system`'s `0x40` (`tsel=40,0,1`), LEON, down to HARD and its confirm on 450 (`hard_mode` and `0x20000`: `tsel=20040,1,1`), and the hand-off on 720. The run compares `ti,tlogo,tfade,tsel,snd`; every field matches through 720; frames 0.03.
- `newgame_arrange_easy.txt`: ARRANGE MODE, CLAIRE, right to EASY and its confirm on 470 (`tsel=20060,0,1`), and the hand-off on 740. Every field, `tsel` included, matches through 740; frames 0.06 (the original's 478 repeats 477, 14).
- `newgame_back.txt`: into ORIGINAL MODE's choice, both characters, EXIT back to the rows on 490, then ARRANGE MODE, LEON, its difficulty down to HARD and back up, the cancel key back to the rows on 750, and a cancel on the rows, which does nothing. Every field matches over the 900 ticks; frames 0.05 (the original's 751 repeats 750).

A recording of play can start from any choice: `oracle-re2.py record` (and `scenario` and `pixels`) take `--difficulty normal|easy|hard` and `--arrange`. The title route then takes two downs to ARRANGE MODE (at 200 and 240, its confirm at 280), and on the difficulty's rows down to HARD or right to EASY at 440, 40 ticks after the character's confirm, with the difficulty's confirm at 480; NORMAL's route is the one it always was. When play begins the tool prints `fg_system`'s choice bits, `hard_mode` and `censorship_off`, and exits with an error if they aren't the request's: the difficulty confirm's `0x20000`, EASY's `0x20` and ARRANGE MODE's `0x40` (`choice: fg_system 20040, hard_mode 1, censorship_off 1` for ARRANGE MODE's HARD). arklay takes the same two options (`--difficulty`, `--arrange`) and applies them before the first room's boot, as the title's hand-off does, and a runner run takes them as `difficulty = "hard"` and `arrange = true`, for both engines. They aren't a setup script's, which runs after the room's boot. The longer routes leave a different `Rnd` when play begins (58231 for HARD, 9202 for ARRANGE MODE's HARD), so a run that compares `rng` sets the reference's.

The zombies under the choice (`oracle/re2-regress.d/hard-mode-zombies.toml`) are `stand_11b`, `stand_610` and `run_119` recorded again with `--difficulty hard`, and `stand_11b` with `--arrange`. The title's longer routes leave `Rnd` at 58231 (HARD) or 52295 (ARRANGE MODE's NORMAL) when play begins, so each setup sets the reference's. `stand_11b_hard` and `stand_610_hard` match on every tick: `Em10_Init`'s five or three times the life, the quick step and its extra draw, and the bites `Pl_life_down` scales. `run_119_hard` matches through several bites to the player's death in a zombie's hands and the game over's title on 2018, where the run ends. On the way, a held player's presses shorten the hold (`Gacha_ch`, `Key_trg & 0x34F`). That needs `Pad_set`'s table: its row 0 gives the action button (raw `0x80`) the shot's bit `0x40` as well as the action's and the confirm's, so an action press counts. `stand_11b_arrange` matches until the player dies on 893. From 894 ARRANGE MODE's uncensored `Die` holds the world, which arklay doesn't port.

arklay's `ui/char_select.lua` opens `ui/title.lua` on ORIGINAL MODE's character rows, as the confirm leaves them, with no white from the boot; the hand-off passes the character and the choice (`api:exit("new_game", character, {easy, hard_mode, censorship_off})`) on to the game, which starts with them as `FUN_004b73c0` and `Title_game_init` do (`docs/internals/deviations/re2.md`, "The new-game choice").

## The regression runner

The original is deterministic, so a recording of it is made once and kept. `scripts/re2-regress.py` plays the runs in `oracle/re2-regress.toml`, and then those in the files of `oracle/re2-regress.d/` in name order, in arklay and compares each with its stored recording, with no Wine and no new recording. Run it for any change to RE 2's play, and read the lines it prints:

```sh
cargo build --profile test-release
scripts/re2-regress.py --out target/re2-regress              # every run, 3 at a time
scripts/re2-regress.py --out target/re2-regress item_take inv_mix   # named runs
scripts/re2-regress.py --out DIR --list                             # the runs and whether their references exist
```

`--out` is required and must not be under `/tmp`, a small RAM disk on the machines this runs on. The binary is `target/test-release/arklay` unless `--arklay` says otherwise. The pack is `--pack`, or else converted into the output dir from `--game` or `ARKLAY_RE2_ROOT` (about 25 seconds). A pack the runner converted is kept with a stamp of the arklay binary and the files under `games/re2`, and is converted again when either changes, since the pack holds the converter's output and those scripts: an old pack runs the enemies' old Lua. Without `--game` or `ARKLAY_RE2_ROOT` a stale pack is used, with a warning. The references are in `--refs`, else `ARKLAY_RE2_REFS`, else `~/.local/share/arklay/re2-refs`: one folder per recording, with `ref.trace` and, where the run compares pictures, `frames/` (PNG, boot frames first), and a `meta.txt` with the input's checksum, the record command and the date. `--jobs` sets the parallelism (3 by default). A run with `frames` makes arklay write a BMP for every tick (`--capture-every 1`), which was most of the runner's disk writes. So the runner compares them while arklay runs. The capture folder is emptied first, so frames an earlier run left there (a `--keep` run, a dead runner) are never compared, and a frame this run can't have written (an index past its last tick, or one seen again) fails the run. The runner polls the folder; arklay writes each frame to a temporary name and renames it, so every frame listed is complete. A frame in the run's `frames` window goes to a pool of comparer processes shared by the jobs (half the cores, at most four); the others are only counted. A compared frame is deleted once 32 newer ones are, unless it is one of the three worst. arklay writes a frame in a millisecond or two and a comparison takes several times that, so when more than 48 frames wait the runner pauses arklay (`SIGSTOP`) until 16 do (`SIGCONT`): a run holds a few tens of MB of frames at most, and arklay's results don't depend on its pace. The capture folder is `/dev/shm/arklay-regress-<pid>/<name>.frames` on Linux, so the frames never reach the disk, when `/dev/shm` has room for every job's bounded backlog; else, and on other systems, it is the out folder. arklay is paused wherever the system has `SIGSTOP`, so on Windows alone the backlog isn't bounded (it is on the disk there, as before). A run that fails moves the frames left (the newest and the worst) to `out/<name>.frames`. The folder is removed when the runner ends, by an error, Ctrl-C, `SIGTERM` or `SIGHUP` too, each of which kills the running arklays; a runner killed outright (`SIGKILL`, an out-of-memory kill) leaves its folder, which the next runner removes at start-up, killing any arklay still writing into it. The results are the same as comparing the finished folder: both go through `frame_error`. With `--keep` the frames are written to the out folder, compared after arklay exits and left there, as are the traces. `re2-regress.py --self-test` checks the streaming comparison against the post-hoc one on a fake capture folder (a stale one, with back-pressure; it needs Pillow). Otherwise arklay's traces are deleted after the comparison.

Each run prints one line: the trace verdict (`MATCH` and the number of ticks compared, or the number of matching ticks and the reference tick and fields of the first difference), the frames' mean error and three worst ticks when the run has frames (and the mean with the message box blanked, where the run has one), and `[ok]` or `[UNEXPECTED: ...]`. The last line is a summary, and the exit status is non-zero when any run is unexpected, a reference is missing (unless `--skip-missing`) or arklay fails. A run is `ok` when its trace matches the manifest's `expect`: `"match"`, or a known first divergence `{ tick, fields, note }` with the tick in the reference's numbering. `compare_from = TICK` starts the comparison at a later reference tick, for a run whose start isn't comparable yet (a scene not ported) but whose rest is; an `ignore` entry that ends before it is refused as stale, and the frames (`frames`, `frame_windows`) keep their own ticks, so a run that starts its trace later says in `frames` where its pictures are comparable. `ignore = [[tick, "field"], ...]` leaves out differences that are known and one tick long, such as `rng` on a door's tick (see the doors above), so that what comes after them still counts. `frame_max_mean` fails a run whose mean frame error grows beyond it. `frame_windows = [[first, last, max_mean], ...]` judges each window of ticks on its own frames, so a part of a run that is drawn can be held to a threshold while the rest is not; the line then lists each window's mean (`scripts/re2-regress.py --self-test`, which the session tier runs, checks the judge). A divergence that comes from a change is a regression: look at it with `oracle.py compare` and the `--keep`ed trace. One that is fixed makes its run `UNEXPECTED` until its `expect` is updated, which is the point: the manifest then says what is known.

A run that names `fgitem`, `fgroom`, `fgscn` or `fgstat` against a reference that carries it on no compared tick (one recorded before the field existed) fails with `the reference has no FIELD (recorded before it existed): record it again or drop the field`; no other field is checked, since `em` and others are legitimately absent from some ticks. A run starts arklay at the original's tick 344 (`offset`), so its input is the original's moved earlier (`input_shift`, or an `_arklay` file) and its setup script, a `.lua` in `oracle/inputs-re2/` or a `setup` table of the position, angle, health, `Rnd`, camera cut and flags that the runner writes out, puts the player where the original has them. Each `3:` entry is dropped from `snd` in both traces first, as in "Weapons", unless the run sets `keep_sound_3`. A run with `voice_from_ref` hands arklay the reference's voice lengths: the runner measures how many ticks each line held `fgstat`'s `0x20` in `ref.trace`, writes them one a line, and passes the file as `--voice-ticks` (with `--sound`), so a line ends on the reference's tick whatever pace it was recorded at ("Sound"). The runs cover the pick-ups, the inventory, the status screen and the weapons, the doors, route walks, runs and stairs from room 100 to room 200, the zombies' stands in rooms 610 and 11B, Kendo's scene, the crouching pick-up, the whole route in one run and the title. A run with `ui = "title"` plays arklay's `--ui title` against a `--boot` record ("The title"), with no setup script. Marvin's turn in room 202 (`marvin_202`) and room 200's entry scene (`scene_200`) are runs too, from references recorded before the runner kept their input; both had no input. A run's `ignore` lists the reference ticks and fields that differ for a known reason: `[tick, "field"]`, or `[first, last, "field"]` for a run of ticks (a range that ends before it starts stops the runner). A matching run's line says how many ticks had an ignored field that really differed (`not counting N known differences`). The six door runs (`door_101_102` and its siblings) ignore `fgroom` from their first compared tick to the one before the room loads: the original's recording starts in room 100 with 5:6 and 5:14 still up from the room before, and a setup script can't hold them, since the room's start clears the room bank as the original's load does (45 ticks on, or 135 with `--sound`, where arklay clears it on the same tick).

`scripts/re2-regress.py record NAME...` records a run's missing reference with `oracle-re2.py record` (it needs `--game` or `ARKLAY_RE2_ROOT`, and on Linux Wine and a display; it uses the Wine prefix, or on Windows the game's tree, under `--work`, or `wine` in `--out`), using the run's `record` arguments from the manifest and `--shot-dir` when the run has frames. It writes `ref.trace`, the sound's files and `frames/` into the refs dir and `meta.txt` beside them, and refuses to replace a reference unless `--force` is given. A `--sound` run with a voice line in it (Kendo's scene) is reproduced on another machine, or on the other system, only at the stored recording's pace: run `oracle-re2.py record` with `--pace` and the reference's `.ticks` (see "On Windows"). arklay runs with a save folder of its own under `--out`, emptied first and removed after (kept with `--keep`), so a run never lists or writes the user's saves. The record starts with the game's save folder empty; a run with `saves = true` keeps what it saved in the reference's `saves/` (listed in `meta.txt`), and one with `saves_from = "<run>"` starts with that reference's `saves/` ("The save screen"); arklay's side doesn't take them yet (the original's `.BIOHAZARD2` files), so the runner refuses to replay such a run. `--dry-run` prints the command. To add a run, put it in a file of its own in `oracle/re2-regress.d/` (named after the run or its family, holding only `[[run]]` tables, which take the main file's `defaults` and field sets), so that two branches adding runs don't both append to the same lines, with its `record` arguments, record it, and run it: put the result you see, if it is a match or one of the divergences this file or `docs/internals/deviations/re2.md` explains, in `expect`.

## Limits

- Runs start from a new game in room 100. There is no way yet to boot a room or
  load a save directly, so other rooms are reached by playing to them.
- Scenario B has no route yet.
- `fg_status`'s top bit differs between runs on the title screen, before tick 0.
  The `--boot` traces leave `fg_status` out.
